# Progress log

The single place a new session reads first (after `CLAUDE.md`). Keep it current: what is done, what is in progress, what is next, what is open.

## Status

**Phase 0 (Blueprint): approved by the founder on 2026-09-22 (defaults accepted for Q1–Q8).**

**M1 Foundations: complete** (report: `docs/reports/M1-founder-report.md`). **M2 Core accounting: complete** (report: `docs/reports/M2-founder-report.md`). **M3 Inventory: complete** (report: `docs/reports/M3-founder-report.md`). **M4 Procure to pay: complete** (report: `docs/reports/M4-founder-report.md`). **M5 Order to cash: in progress** (founder go-ahead 2026-09-24). Development environment note: this session runs on Ubuntu 24.04 with .NET 10.0.112 SDK (apt), Node 22 + pnpm, a local PostgreSQL 16 cluster and Docker (image pulls from Docker Hub are blocked by the egress policy, so tests use the `QUICKER_TEST_CONNECTION` override instead of Testcontainers here; CI uses a postgres:17 service container).

Branches: `claude/quicker-erp-founding-arch-4cq18i` (Phase 0 and slices 1.1–1.5), `claude/efficient-request-ajd9zy` (slice 1.6 onwards, built on top of it). Default branch: `main`.

Migration `V0002__identity.sql`. Request pipeline: unit of work per request (middleware) with commit-on-success filter and an explicit `CommitOnFailure` for security bookkeeping; principal resolution cached by permissions epoch; `RequirePermission` and `RequireRecentAuth` endpoint filters; problem details by `ErrorKind`. Tests: 19 API-level tests (sign-up, lockout, refresh reuse, MFA, step-up, reset, invitations, SoD, API keys, cross-tenant 404s, tenant selection, full OIDC flow against an in-process fake provider) plus row factories so the schema suite covers all 9 new tenant tables. |
| 1.5 Audit log | done | Module `Quicker.Audit` and migration `V0003__audit.sql`: per-tenant hash chains in `app.aud_events` (monthly partitions, RLS, append-only; `seq`, `prev_hash` and `hash` assigned by a SECURITY DEFINER trigger under the tenant's head lock, canonical form rendered by `app.aud_canonical`), a platform chain in `control` for events recorded outside any tenant, `control.aud_anchors` and `control.aud_verifications`. EF `SaveChangesInterceptor` captures every insert/update/delete of entities annotated `HasAuditTrail` (Identity: user, membership, role, SoD rule, API key, SSO connection) with before/after/diff, redaction by annotation and by name; the sink buffers per unit of work and writes at commit; explicit events win over captured ones and inherit their diff; anonymous-then-tenant requests carry their events into the tenant. Verifier recomputes every link and checks head and anchor (`ok`/`empty`/`broken`/`truncated`/`anchor_mismatch`); file anchor store (hash-linked JSON lines); `AuditChainJobs` anchors and verifies every tenant. API: record timeline, explorer (keyset paging, filters), event detail, audited JSON-lines export, chain status/verify/anchor, operator-only platform chain. `IUnitOfWork.BeforeCommit` hook and `AddModuleDbContext` (interceptors) in the building blocks. Tests: 24 (row and anchor-file tampering, tail removal and head rewrite detected; every identity mutation produces an event with before/after; secrets absent from exports; platform chain operator-only; app role cannot alter events or heads; all-tenant jobs), and the isolation suite covers the two new tenant tables. |

Companies (functional/reporting currency, time zone, costing, rounding and policy settings; functional currency immutable), branches with their auto-created `BRANCH` dimension value, company currencies (display decimals, cash rounding), typed settings per tenant and per company. Fiscal calendars with 12 or 13 periods, years opened explicitly or automatically for a new company, `FY2026/27` codes, per-company per-module period states (open / soft_closed / hard_closed / never_opened) with the reopen override (reason, recent auth, audited) and the `(company, date, module)` resolver. Rate types (system + `official`/`market`), effective-dated rates with correction reasons, resolution direct → inverse → cross through the functional currency, provider import (ECB daily/90-day XML, Open Exchange Rates latest/historical) that never overwrites manual rates and is audited. Dimensions (hierarchical, validity, company scope), dimension sets deduplicated by SHA-256 hash (race-free `ON CONFLICT`), units of measure with rational conversions resolved directly, inversely or through one intermediate unit. Business calendars (working days + holidays) with due-date and working-day arithmetic. Contracts for later modules: `ICompanyDirectory`, `IFiscalPeriodResolver`, `IExchangeRateResolver`, `IWorkingDayCalendar`, `IDimensionSets`, `IUomConversions`. `ITenantSetupStep` (Tenancy contracts) lets modules seed tenant defaults at sign-up; Organization seeds calendars, dimensions, rate types and units. Tests: 17 (July fiscal year with 13 periods and date resolution, period states and reopen audit, defaults at sign-up, branch → dimension value, company currencies and settings, direct/inverse/cross rates incl. the Iraq official/market case, corrections with reasons, ECB and OXR import against a local stub, due dates skipping Friday/Saturday and holidays, dimension sets, unit conversions, provider parsers) plus row factories so the isolation suite covers all 17 tables. |

Templates: `{company} {branch} {yy} {yyyy} {mm} {fy}` and one `{seq}`/`{seq:N}`, validated at save; the sequence is rendered by the database so the stored text equals what the caller received. Selection: most specific series wins (branch > fiscal year > default), explicit override to any applicable series. `INumberAllocator` (allocate/preview) for documents, `DraftIdentifiers` (`DRAFT-xxxxxxxx`). Admin API: series CRUD (audited by capture; gapless/reset/type frozen once numbers exist), counters per period with the audited backwards move (`numbering.counter.reset` + reason, never below an issued number), allocation log, gapless audit report (missing numbers per period), preview and allocation for external documents. Tests: 18 — selection and templates, validation codes, 50 concurrent allocations consecutive with no gap or duplicate, rollback reuses the number, immutability of the log for the application role, yearly (July fiscal year) and monthly resets, counter moves with permission and audit, template engine unit tests — plus row factories for the 3 tables. |
| 1.8 Platform services | done | **(a) Messaging core: done.** Migration `V0006__platform.sql` (`ops.outbox_messages` with an identity sequence, `ops.inbox`, `ops.jobs`, `ops.schedules`, `ops.idempotency_keys`, NOTIFY triggers) and seed `S0002__platform_schedules.sql` (daily audit anchoring and verification, outbox archive, idempotency sweep). `Quicker.Messaging` now holds: `IOutbox` (writes integration events inside the unit of work), `IIntegrationEventHandler<T>` with a registry built from DI, the dispatcher (SKIP LOCKED claims in insertion order, one message in flight per aggregate so order holds per aggregate, handlers in their own tenant-bound unit of work with the inbox row committed alongside the effect, exponential backoff 1 s → 1 h, dead letters after 20 attempts, operator list/retry, archive), `IJobQueue` and `IJobHandler<T>` (claim with SKIP LOCKED by priority, final state written in the job's own transaction, heartbeat on a side connection, stale reclaim, `JobFailedException` for permanent failures, retries with backoff then dead, idempotency keys per tenant and type, progress reporting, tenant/operator admin), a five-field cron evaluator with time zones, the scheduler (leader by advisory lock, arms new schedules without firing the past, one job per slot by idempotency key, missed slots collapse), LISTEN/NOTIFY wake-up with polling fallback, and the hosted loops. `Quicker.Worker` host (+ `deploy/docker/worker.Dockerfile`, Compose service, `make up`); the API can embed the loops with `Quicker:Worker:Embedded=true`. Jobs registered: `audit.anchor_all`, `audit.verify_all` (fails when a chain is broken), `organization.rates.import` (per tenant), `ops.outbox_archive`, `ops.idempotency_sweep`. API: `/api/v1/platform/jobs` (list, get, types, retry, cancel), `/platform/schedules` (tenant), `/platform/ops/*` (operators: outbox by state, retry dead letters, platform schedules). Tests: 29 — kill-the-worker for events (30 events, crash mid-batch, restart: every effect exactly once, order per aggregate, redelivery a no-op), two workers sharing the queue, backoff → dead letter → operator retry, archive; jobs on three slots surviving a crash with one effect each, retry/backoff/dead/permanent/retry, idempotency keys, heartbeat and reclaim, tenant API isolation; cron semantics (Vixie OR rule, time zones, leap day), scheduler arming/firing/slot idempotency/leader lock, seeded platform schedules and the audit jobs end to end; the hosted loops woken by NOTIFY with polling disabled. **(b1) Idempotency and webhooks: done.** `IdempotencyMiddleware` (Quicker.Web): mutating requests with `Idempotency-Key` are scoped to tenant + principal, hashed (method, path, query, body), serialised on an advisory lock, and their final response (status, body, content type; not 5xx) is stored in `ops.idempotency_keys` for 24 hours; a replay returns it with `Idempotent-Replayed: true`, a different request under the same key is refused (`idempotency.key_reused`), `Quicker:Api:Idempotency:Required` turns the header mandatory. Module `Quicker.Integration` (migration `V0007__integration.sql`): webhook subscriptions (name, URL, event-type patterns `sales.*`/`*`, filters, active; secret generated once, stored encrypted with the platform key via the new `ISecretProtector` contract, rotatable), the outbox observer `WebhookFanout` (a new `IIntegrationEventObserver` sees every event) creating a delivery per matching subscription, the job `integration.webhook.deliver` posting the envelope `{id, type, version, occurredAt, tenantId, aggregateType, aggregateId, data}` with `X-Quicker-Signature: v1=HMAC-SHA256(secret, "<timestamp>.<body>")`, timestamp, event id/type, delivery id and attempt headers, retries with backoff for about a day (30 attempts) with the delivery log written independently of the job's rollback, dead after that; API for subscriptions, test delivery (`webhook.test`), delivery log and replay. `IMemberDirectory` contract added to Identity for the modules that follow. Tests: 4 (fan-out by pattern with signature verification, isolation, inactive subscriptions; 500 → retry → delivered, replay, test delivery, secret rotation, validation, audit redaction; idempotent replay/refusal/errors; 10 concurrent replays of one key create one company). **(b2) Notifications, email, storage: done.** Migration `V0008__collaboration.sql` and module `Quicker.Collaboration`: `INotifier` (contract) writes in-app rows (`col_notifications`, bilingual title/body, link, entity, data) and queues emails per recipient after their channel preferences (`col_notification_preferences`: per kind or `*`, in-app/email); `collaboration.email.send` jobs send through `IEmailSender` and record every attempt in `col_email_log` (status queued/sent/failed, retries with backoff, 10 attempts); API `/api/v1/collaboration/notifications` (my inbox with cursor paging, unread count, mark read/read-all, preferences, `announce` to every active member under `collaboration.notification.announce`). Email providers (`Quicker:Email:Provider`): `capture` (dev/tests) and `smtp` (`SmtpEmailSender`, STARTTLS and credentials optional; Mailpit in Compose). New building block `Quicker.Storage`: `IObjectStorage` with `filesystem` (temp-then-move writes, sidecar metadata, retention refuses replace/delete) and `s3` (AWS SDK, MinIO in Compose; keys under `immutable/` go to an object-lock bucket in compliance mode and are read back by version id) providers, buckets created on first use. Attachments (`col_attachments`, `/api/v1/collaboration/attachments`: multipart upload spooled with SHA-256 and the `Quicker:Storage:MaxUploadBytes` limit, list per record, download, delete removes the object before commit; audited). Audit anchor store `object_lock` (`Quicker:Audit:Anchoring:Store`, retention `RetentionDays`, default 10 years) writes one retained JSON object per anchor. Tests: 6 (announcement fan-out with preferences, inbox/read/paging, real SMTP session against a fake relay, refusal → retry → sent with the log per attempt, isolation and permissions; attachments end to end incl. limits, isolation, permissions, object removal and audit trail; filesystem retention; object-lock anchors verified and tamper-detected; S3 provider versioning and lock, run where `QUICKER_TEST_S3_ENDPOINT` is set — CI starts MinIO). **(c) Collaboration on records: done.** Migration `V0009__collaboration_2.sql`: comments (`col_comments`: body, @mentions of members, replies, edit and soft delete; author-or-`collaboration.comment.manage` rule; every comment is an activity and every mentioned member gets a `collaboration.mention` notification), the activity timeline (`col_activities`, written by any module through `IActivityLog` with bilingual summaries and data; attachments, comments and links write it), document links (`col_document_links`, `IDocumentLinks`: directed, typed relations such as `source`/`fulfils`/`reverses`, idempotent per (from, to, relation), listed from either end, both timelines updated), saved views (`col_saved_views`: per entity type, private unless shared, one default per owner, definition object limited to filters/sort/columns/groupBy/pageSize/density, owner-or-`collaboration.view.manage` rule) and custom-field definitions (`col_custom_fields`: key, bilingual label, type text/number/date/boolean/select/multi_select/reference, required, options, rules min/max/maxLength/pattern/referenceType, position, active, indexed). Hosts register the jsonb column they carry (`CustomFieldHosts`; `company` → `app.org_companies.custom_fields` is the first), `ICustomFieldValidator` normalises and validates values on every create/update (`custom_field.required/unknown/value_invalid` with `why`), `indexed=true` creates `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS <table>_cf_<key>_idx ON <table> (tenant_id, ((custom_fields->>'<key>')))` on the owner connection just before the definition's commit (dropped when unset or deleted), and `GET /collaboration/custom-fields/{entityType}/schema` publishes a JSON Schema (draft 2020-12) of the fields. API under `/api/v1/collaboration`: `comments`, `activities`, `links`, `views`, `custom-fields`. Tests: 4 more (comments/mentions/timeline/authorship/isolation; links from both ends with idempotency and timelines; views private/shared/default/ownership/validation; custom fields end to end on companies including the physical index in `pg_indexes`, schema, identity lock, audit trail and tenant separation). |
| 1.9 API skeleton | done | **(a) Contract, client, limits: done.** OpenAPI 3.1 is generated at build (`Microsoft.Extensions.ApiDescription.Server`, `contracts/openapi-v1.json`, committed; `make api-contract` regenerates it and the client) with stable operation ids (`getOrganizationCompaniesByCompanyId`); endpoints return typed results (`ApiProblems.Ok/Created/Accepted/NoContent/Found` unions of the success type and `ProblemHttpResult`), so 125 of 185 operations carry their response schema and 123 DTOs are in the contract. Web: `openapi-typescript` generates `apps/web/src/api/schema.d.ts` and `openapi-fetch` gives the typed client (`src/api/client.ts`: bearer and Accept-Language middleware, problem-details helper); ESLint forbids raw `fetch` outside `src/api`, and the shell uses the client. CI: the .NET job fails when the built contract differs from the committed one; the web job regenerates the client and fails on drift, and `tools/check-diagrams/check-api-contract.mjs` fails on breaking changes against `main` (removed path/operation/response status/response property/enum value/parameter, request property removed or newly required). Rate limits (`Quicker:Api:RateLimit`): sliding windows per principal (600/min), per address for anonymous calls (120/min) and for `/api/v1/auth/*` (20/min), `429` problem `rate.limited` with `Retry-After`; off in the test fixture except its own test. Problem details with `code` and `why` were already in place (ADR-0012). **(b) Paging, filtering, shaping, validators: done.** `Page<T> {items, nextCursor}` with opaque base64url cursors and keyset paging on version-7 ids (`KeysetPaging`, `?limit=&cursor=`; `page.cursor_invalid` on a foreign cursor) on notifications, activities and webhook deliveries (audit events already paged by `seq`). The filter language (`FilterParser`/`FilterSpec<T>`: `field op value [and …]`, ops eq/ne/gt/ge/lt/le/like/in/isnull/notnull, quoted strings, numbers, booleans, dates; per-endpoint field whitelists; `cf.<key>` on custom fields through `JsonFunctions.Text` → `jsonb_extract_path_text`, the same expression the custom-field index is built on) on companies (`?filter=country eq 'IQ' and cf.region eq 'north'`; `filter.syntax/field_unknown/value_invalid` problems list the allowed fields). Field selection `?fields=code,country` on every GET (object, array or page items; `id` always kept) and `?expand=branches` on a company. Validators: every GET answers with an `ETag` (weak, body hash) and `304` on `If-None-Match`; a company's GET sets its version ETag (`W/"v<ticks>"`) and PUT with `If-Match` refuses a stale version with `412 precondition.failed`. Tests: 1 more (filters incl. custom fields and errors, field selection, expansion, 304/412, per-tenant list validators). |

i18next with ICU (Arabic plural categories) and `en.json`/`ar.json` (238 keys; `pnpm check-i18n` fails on a missing Arabic key), language switch flips `lang` and `dir` on the document, per-user digit preference (Western or Eastern Arabic through `Intl` with `nu-arab`), light/dark theme; Playwright (`pnpm --filter @quicker/web e2e`, `E2E_CHROMIUM_PATH` for a pre-installed browser): the English journey (workspace → custom field → company refused until the required field is set → command palette finds it → invitation → announcement in the inbox → shortcuts overlay) and the Arabic journey (RTL document, sidebar on the right, bilingual company, Eastern Arabic digits) with axe (WCAG 2.2 AA, no serious or critical violation) on every screen; CI job `e2e` runs them against the migrated API. |
| 1.11 Observability | done | `Quicker.Observability` (both hosts call `AddQuickerObservability`): Serilog structured JSON on the console (compact format, `service` and `environment` properties, levels from the `Serilog` section, request lines carrying the trace id and every `quicker.*` span tag), OpenTelemetry traces (ASP.NET Core minus health probes, HttpClient, Npgsql, and the `Quicker.Requests`/`Quicker.Jobs`/`Quicker.Outbox` sources) and metrics (ASP.NET Core and HttpClient histograms, Npgsql pool, the `Quicker.Platform` meter: `quicker.requests` by tenant and actor, gauges `quicker.outbox.lag`, `quicker.outbox.pending`, `quicker.jobs.overdue`, `quicker.jobs.dead`, `quicker.jobs.running` sampled every 15 s) exported over OTLP whenever `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT` is set; every request span is tagged with tenant, user, membership, actor type and request id (ids only, no PII), every job and event handler runs in its own span with type, attempt and tenant. Health as minimal-API endpoints in the contract: `/health/live`, `/health/ready` (database and migrations, outbox lag under `Quicker:Health:MaxOutboxLagSeconds` = 300, overdue jobs under `MaxOverdueJobs` = 1000; 503 with the failing check named) and `/health/deps` (every check with its data). Local profile `make observe`: `grafana/otel-lgtm` (Grafana, Tempo, Prometheus, Loki, OTLP collector on 4317/4318) layered over Compose, API and worker exporting to it. Tests: 2 (a request trace with tenant/user/membership tags and an Npgsql child span; readiness 503 on a two-hour-old unpublished message with the outbox check and lag reported, healthy again after, `/health/deps` shape). |
| 1.12 Demo seed v1 | done | `Quicker.Migrator demo` (`make demo`) rebuilds the demo tenant, `all` (Compose, `make up`, CI) creates it only when missing. The seeder composes the modules exactly as the hosts do (no HTTP, no background loops) and drives their own services inside one unit of work, so every company, branch, currency, role assignment and rate type goes through the same validation and audit trail as an administrator's request; only users (no invitation round-trip), memberships and the 1,525 rate rows are written through the module contexts directly. Dataset (ASSUMPTIONS A-087): tenant `demo` with a fixed version-7 id; three companies — `IQT` Al-Rafidain Trading (IQD functional, USD reporting, Baghdad/Basra/Erbil branches, IQD cash rounding 250), `USI` Tigris Import (USD functional, IQD reporting, Baghdad), `AEG` Gulf Gate (AED functional, USD reporting, Dubai, AED rounding 0.25) with bilingual names, registrations and addresses; ten users at `@quicker.example` sharing the password `DemoPass2026!`, one per default role (owner, admin, accountant in Arabic with Eastern Arabic digits, AR and AP clerks, purchaser, sales rep and warehouse operator scoped to `IQT`, approver, auditor); rate types `official` and `market` next to the system four; 365 days ending today (Baghdad) of spot USD/IQD (bounded random walk 1450–1560 from a fixed seed), market USD/IQD, spot EUR/USD and AED/IQD, the pegged USD/AED, the official CBI 1310, month-end closing and monthly average rates. Reseeding purges the tenant physically (children before parents from the foreign keys, maintenance mode for the append-only audit chain, the demo users, then the tenant row) and rebuilds it with the same tenant, user and membership ids in about 6 s locally. Fix on the way: the `accountant` template granted `accounting.*`, which the default block rule (post journals versus reopen periods) made unassignable on its own; it now names the accounting areas and `accounting.period.manage` without `reopen`. Tests: 2 in `tests/Demo` (seed on a migrated database under the one-minute budget, then a plain run keeps the tenant and its ids, then a reseed purges a queued job and the audit chain and recreates the same ids; identifiers and the rate series are identical across runs, version-7 layout kept). Also fixed on the way: `WorkerLoopTests.Notify_wakes…` read the outbox between a handler's commit and the dispatcher's batch commit and failed under load; it now waits for both within its deadline. |


### M2 slices

| Slice | Status | Notes |
|-------|--------|-------|
| 2.1 Chart of accounts | done | `Quicker.Accounting` module (Contracts, module, TestSupport, Tests; V0010, seed S0003). Charts shared by the tenant or dedicated to one company, with a code format (`####`); the account tree with types, subtypes, categories (statement mapping, seeded per tenant), header/postable, control flags with subledger type (AR, AP, INV, FA, BANK, PDC, GRNI, IC, WHT), currency restriction, manual-posting flag, FX revaluation flag, cash-flow category, a default posting role and an optional owning company; every structural rule enforced (code format, unique code, parent is a header of the same type, no cycles, control needs a subledger, header cannot be control, a header with children cannot become postable, children keep the parent's type). Dimension rules per account (required, optional with default value, blocked) and `POST /accounts/{id}/check`: the same check the posting engine will run (`IChartOfAccounts.CheckLineAsync`): chart of the company, active, postable, company scope, currency restriction, manual posting, required/blocked dimensions, defaults applied. Templates: `IFRS_SME` (109 accounts, bilingual, a default account for every one of the 61 posting roles of POSTING_RULES §1, control accounts closed to manual posting), `GCC` (VAT input/output, Zakat payable and expense), `IRAQ_UAS` (the same layout with every postable account mapped to a group of the Iraqi Unified Accounting System). Statutory charts as platform reference data (`control.gl_statutory_charts`, seeded) with per-account mappings (`PUT /charts/{id}/mappings/{code}`). CSV export (16 fixed columns, RFC 4180) and import (CSV or JSON, upsert by code, parents may follow children, all or nothing with the failing row named). `PUT /accounting/companies/{id}/chart` assigns the chart through the new `ICompanyDirectory.AssignChartAsync` (Organization keeps `org_companies.chart_id`, now a real foreign key); `POST /charts/from-template {companyId}` does it in one call so a new company posts on day one. New Organization contracts: `IDimensionDirectory`, `IDimensionSets.GetAsync`, `CompanyInfo.ChartId`. Permissions `accounting.chart.read/manage`. Tests: 4 (templates cover every role with the right control flags and bilingual names; a company from a template with the tree, categories, settings, CSV round trip and JSON import errors; 20 structural and dimension-rule refusals through the API plus defaults, company scope and chart assignment rules; statutory mappings from the Iraq template and their edits) plus the schema suite over the five new tables. |
| 2.2 Posting engine | done | V0011: `gl_posting_groups`, `gl_posting_profiles` (versioned per company, draft/active/retired, `org_companies.posting_profile_id` now a real deferrable FK), `gl_posting_rules` (role + nullable keys: document type, item and partner posting groups, tax code, warehouse, branch, bank account, asset category, charge type; specificity stored; one rule per key combination), `gl_journal_entries` (append-only; the reversed state is a link row, not a status column), `gl_journal_lines` (append-only, partitioned by posting year with `app.gl_ensure_partition`, primary key `(tenant_id, posting_date, id)`, three amounts per line with both rates, subledger type/ref checked together), `gl_entry_links` (reverses/corrects/auto_reversal_of/closing_of; a unique index makes a second reversal impossible), `gl_balances` (company × account × period × transaction currency × dimension set, nil uuid = none). A deferred constraint trigger on the entry asserts at commit that every declared line was written and debits equal credits in tc, fc and rc, so no client can commit an unbalanced entry (tested with raw SQL as the owner). `IPostingService.PostAsync` (Contracts): balance check before anything else, company/chart/profile resolution, period state with `accounting.period.post_in_soft_closed` (system actors pass), rates through `IExchangeRateResolver` (tc→fc, fc→rc; identity when the reporting currency is the transaction currency, override with reason), `PostingRuleMatcher` (most specific match, `posting.rule_missing` names the role and keys, equal specificity is `posting.rule_ambiguous`), explicit accounts only on manual journals, the BRANCH dimension added from the line or request branch, `IChartOfAccounts.CheckLineAsync` per line (dimension rules with defaults), control cross-check (`posting.subledger_ref_required`, `posting.subledger_type_mismatch`, `posting.subledger_unexpected`), per-line rounding with the company's `RoundingPolicy`, a `RoundingDifferences` line when fc or rc totals differ, dimension sets, a default gapless series `JE-{yyyy}-{seq:6}` per company through the new `INumberAllocator.EnsureDefaultSeriesAsync`, balances upserted under the row lock, audit `posted`, outbox event `accounting.journal.posted`, idempotent replay by key. `ReverseAsync`: mirror entry with all three currencies copied, on the original date when its GL period is open for the actor, else the first day of the first open period (`IFiscalPeriodResolver.FirstOpenPeriodAsync`), links both ways, refuses a second reversal and reversals of reversals. `JournalService`: entry with lines/links, paged entries per company, balance rows, `verify` (stored versus rebuilt, differences per column) and `rebuild` (delete and recompute in one transaction, audited); `Quicker.Migrator rebuild-balances --tenant SLUG [--company CODE]`. Profiles API: groups, `from-chart` (one default rule per role from the chart's default accounts, activated, `unresolvedRoles` for the wizard), rule replacement with control-role validation, activation retiring the previous version. Permissions `accounting.profile.read/manage`, `accounting.journal.read/post/reverse`, `accounting.period.post_in_soft_closed`, `accounting.balance.rebuild`. Tests: 3 matcher unit tests over the determination matrix and 4 API tests (USD sale in an AED company with a USD reporting currency: 367.29 versus 367.28 and the 0.01 rounding line, replay, entry and balances read back; 13 refusals with their codes and facts including the required cost centre and the hard-closed period; raw SQL entries: balanced commits, unbalanced and incomplete are rejected at commit, posted lines refuse updates even for the owner; reversal into October after September is closed with mirrored lines and links, AP netting to zero, verify clean, a corrupted balance row found by verify and repaired by rebuild). |
| 2.3 Journals | done | V0012: `gl_manual_journals` (draft → pending_approval → approved → posted, or rejected/cancelled; kinds manual, opening, accrual, reversing, allocation, recurring, deferral; number `DRAFT-…` until posting, then `MJ-{yyyy}-{seq:5}` from a per-company gapless series created on first use), `gl_manual_journal_lines` (account, debit or credit, dimensions, subledger type and item, bilingual description), `gl_recurring_templates` (cron in the company's time zone, starts/ends, fixed, percentage or variable amounts, review flag, next run), `gl_deferral_schedules` and `gl_deferral_lines` (prepayment, accrual, deferred revenue; straight-line or daily; one planned line per fiscal period, or per calendar month when the company has no fiscal periods yet). `ManualJournalService`: every line has one side and a rounded amount, accounts resolved by code on the company's chart, headers refused, a control account must carry its subledger item (`journal.subledger_pair`), accruals name their reversal date (`journal.auto_reverse_date`); submit is balanced only unless the kind is `opening`; the company setting `accounting.journals.approval` (`required` or `none`, tenant default `none`) decides whether submit waits for approval; the submitter cannot approve (`journal.approve_own`) and rejection needs a reason; posting goes through `IPostingService` with `isManual`, the journal's number as the entry's source number and the entry id kept on the journal, idempotent by journal; an opening journal posts with `isOpeningEntry` and the engine balances it on the `OpeningBalanceEquity` role. The posting engine now lets a manual line touch a control account when the line names its subledger item (POSTING_RULES §8 unchanged for lines without one). CSV/JSON import groups rows by `journal_ref`, all or nothing with the failing journal and row named. `RecurringService`: templates validated (cron, balanced, minor-unit amounts, percentage shares summing to 100 with the last credit line absorbing the rounding of the base amount), `generate` for the next due run or a date, and `GenerateDueAsync` for every run due up to a date; the generated journal posts at once unless the template requires review or is variable. `DeferralService`: amortisation to the minor unit with the last line taking the remainder (1000.01 over 12 → 11 × 83.33 + 83.38; daily 1000.01 over Jan–Feb → 525.43 + 474.58), preview without saving, `post-due` posting the planned lines on or before a date (Dr target / Cr balance account, reversed for deferred revenue; a closed period leaves the line waiting with the reason), cancel of the unposted remainder. `AccountingRoutines` (`POST /routines/run`, and the platform schedule `accounting.daily` → job `accounting.daily_routines` at 00:30 UTC fanning out over active tenants): automatic reversals of entries whose `auto_reverse_on` has come (linked `auto_reversal_of`, reason "Automatic reversal", first open period when the date is closed), recurring generation, deferral postings; every item that cannot run is returned as waiting with its problem code. `ICompanySettings` (Organization contract: company value over tenant value). Permissions `accounting.journal.manage`, `accounting.journal.approve` (sensitive), `accounting.routine.run` (sensitive). Tests: 4 API tests (lifecycle with approval required then not, the rules on the way, posting once with the MJ number on the entry, a control account with and without its subledger item; opening balances balanced to 3400 and an accrual reversed by the routine on its date and not before; a monthly template generating October and November in one run and advancing to December, a percentage template landing 100.01 on both sides as a draft for review, a 12-period prepayment previewed, posted to 2026-12-31 (4 lines, 333.32) and cancelled; CSV import grouped by reference with an unknown account rolling everything back, JSON import) plus the schema suite over the five new tables (200 tests in all, the S3 one skipped without MinIO). |
| 2.4 Periods and locks | done | Built on the 1.6 period machinery (states per company and module, soft/hard close, reopen with `accounting.period.reopen`, a reason, recent authentication and an `override` audit event) and the 2.2 engine checks (`period.closed` / `period.soft_closed` with the period, its state and the permission named; reversals into the first open period). V0013 adds **posting windows** (`org_posting_windows`): a company's allow-posting-from/to dates for everyone or per role (`GET|PUT /organization/companies/{id}/posting-windows`, `accounting.period.manage`, audited); the engine holds every person to the effective window (the widest of their roles' windows, else the company's; system actors exempt) and refuses with `posting.outside_window` naming the bounds and the role; the principal now carries its active role ids and Identity exposes `IRoleDirectory`. **Corrections** (scenario 7): `IPostingService.CorrectAsync` reverses an entry into the first open period on or after its date and posts the replacement there (or on its own later date) in one transaction, the replacement linked `corrects` → original next to the reversal's `reverses`, audited `corrected`; `POST /journal-entries/{id}/correct {reason, replacement}` for documents, and for manual journals `POST /journals/{id}/correct {reason}` drafts a copy of the posted journal in the first open period (`correctsJournalId`, `correctionReason`), editable and approvable like any draft; posting it runs the correction and the original shows `correctedByJournalId`; a journal is corrected once and a reversed entry cannot be corrected. Fixed on the way, platform-wide: the request unit of work committed refused requests whose endpoint returns a typed union (`Results<Ok<T>, ProblemHttpResult>`, most endpoints since 1.9) because the union carries no status code; the filter now looks at the wrapped result, so a refused posting rolls back the gapless number it had taken (asserted). Tests: 1 scenario test (September posted and hard-closed; a September posting refused with the reopen permission named; the correction drafted in October, edited, posted; reversal and replacement both dated 1 October with links in both directions; a second correction refused; reopen refused for a clerk, done by the owner with a reason and read back from the audit timeline with the actor; the clerk's role windowed to 15 October: 5 October refused with the bounds, 20 October posted, the owner unrestricted until a company window is set, and then the role window still governs its holders) plus the schema suite over the new table (201 tests in all). |
| 2.5 Inquiry and reports | done | **API:** `InquiryService` under `/companies/{id}/reports`: **trial balance** at any date (`asOf`, today in the company's time zone by default) with an optional movement window (`from`: opening, debit, credit, closing per account), a comparative (`compareAsOf`, the same window length earlier), basis `fc` or `rc`, `groupBy=DIMENSION` (one row per account and dimension value, lines without the dimension in their own row), dimension filters `d.CODE=valueId`, closing entries excluded unless `includeClosing`; `balanced` is a zero opening total with equal movements; every row carries `drill` (account, window, filters) and the ledger over those parameters closes on the row's figure. **Account ledger** (`accountId` or `accountCode`, `from`, `to`, same filters): opening, running balance, paged by a keyset cursor on (date, entry number, line) with the balance carried across pages, every line with its entry, source document type, id, number and `sourceLink` where the platform knows the document (manual journals, journal entries; documents of M3+ register theirs); a line without its own description shows its entry's. **Balances by dimension** (`dimension-balances?dimension=&accountType=&accountId=`). **Journal browser** filters on the entries list: number prefix (entry or source document), an account on any line, manual only, minimum total, free text over descriptions and numbers. **Exports** `format=csv|xlsx` on the trial balance and the ledger (`Quicker.Web.Exports.TabularExport`: RFC 4180 CSV with a byte order mark; XLSX written as a minimal SpreadsheetML package with real number and date cells, bold headers, right-to-left sheet under an Arabic Accept-Language, no spreadsheet library), audited as `exported`. Amounts are read from the journal lines, not the derived balances, so the two sources are checked against each other by the invariant harness (2.6). Also on the way: `POST /charts/from-template` with a company now creates that company's first posting profile too (the day-one promise held only with a profile), and the engine resolves a back-dated entry against the profile version that governed its date after a newer version took over (retired versions stay effective for their dates; drafts never). **Web pass** (`apps/web/src/routes/accounting`, six screens in the navigation with `g` shortcuts, all strings in English and Arabic, company remembered per browser): **Chart of accounts** (tree with levels and flags, search, account editor with the control/subledger, manual-posting and currency fields, create-from-template for a company without a chart, dimension rules per account, link to the ledger), **Journals** (list by status, line editor with running totals, submit/approve/reject/post/cancel/correct with reasons, links to the entry and between a journal and its correction), **Trial balance** (date, window, comparative, basis, grouping, one dimension filter, balanced badge, totals, CSV/XLSX download through the client, click a row to open its ledger), **Account ledger** (opening, lines with running balance, entry and source document links, load more, exports), **Journal entries** (browser with filters, the entry with lines, links to related entries and a reversal with reason), **Period control** (every period of a fiscal year with the state per module, close/soft-close/reopen for the chosen modules with a reason, allow-posting windows editor per role). Tests: 1 API test (five journals across the year; the trial balance balances at three sampled dates and every row's drill closes on its figure; window, comparative, filter, grouping; ledger running balance across pages and the source document link; balances by cost centre; browser filters; CSV header, totals and rows; XLSX unzipped and checked for number and date cells; exports in the audit trail), 1 web unit test (the trial balance page renders the report in English and in Arabic with the direction flipped), 1 browser journey with axe on every screen (sign-up, company, chart from template, an account's rules, a journal drafted and posted from the editor, the trial balance balanced and drilled to the ledger, the current period hard-closed and reopened with a reason). |
| 2.6 Invariant harness v1 | done | `Quicker.Integrity` module (Contracts `IInvariantHarness`, `InvariantReport`, `InvariantResult`, `InvariantCodes`; the module is not named `Invariants` because the build treats `*.Invariants` projects as test projects, which `tests/Invariants/Quicker.Invariants` is). Six read-only checks over the current tenant (optionally one company), each with what it examined and the first 20 problems: `entries_balanced` (every entry has its declared lines and debits equal credits in tc, fc and rc), `trial_balance_zero` (per company the lines sum to zero in fc and rc), `balances_match_lines` (`gl_balances` against the lines row by row and column by column, both directions of a full outer join), `audit_chain_intact` (the tenant chain recomputed through the new `IAuditChainVerifier` contract of Audit), `tenant_isolation` (every table of `ops.tenant_tables` carries tenant_id with row security enabled, forced and policed), `gapless_numbering` (each gapless series and period has no missing number and its counter sits after the last one). Three ways to run it: `POST /api/v1/platform/integrity/run?companyId=` (`platform.integrity.run`, sensitive), the platform job `integrity.check_all` (schedule `integrity.daily` at 03:30 UTC over every active tenant in its own unit of work; the job fails naming each failing tenant and its checks, so operators see it like a broken audit chain), and after every accounting scenario test (`AssertInvariantsAsync` on the tenant's client; the one test that writes raw entries as the owner to prove the database's own guard is the only one that does not, because those rows never touched the balances on purpose). Subledger-to-control and inventory-to-GL checks (ADR-0029 items 3 and 4) join when those modules land in M3–M4. Tests: 2 in `tests/Invariants` (intact books pass every check with the counts expected, a balance row altered by the schema owner in maintenance mode fails the harness naming the account, column and both values, and a rebuild makes it pass again; a tampered audit event is named with its sequence, a posted line altered behind the append-only guard fails `entries_balanced`, `trial_balance_zero` and `balances_match_lines` with the amounts, and the platform job fails naming that tenant and its four failing checks) plus the accounting suite now ending every scenario with the harness. |
| 2.7 Demo seed v2 | done | `DemoBooks` extends the same `demo` tenant (ASSUMPTIONS A-095) through the accounting services, inside the seeder's one unit of work, so every journal is validated, numbered, audited and posted exactly as a user's would be. Per company: a chart from the template that fits it (`IQT` → `IRAQ_UAS`, `USI` → `IFRS_SME`, `AEG` → `GCC`) and its first posting profile; cost centres `CC-ADM/SLS/OPS/LOG`, departments `FIN/HR/IT` and projects `PRJ-ERP/PRJ-BSR` as dimension values next to the branches; six customers and five suppliers as subledger items on the AR and AP control accounts; fiscal years covering the twelve months up to today; an opening journal (bank, cash, fixed assets, capital, a loan), an insurance prepayment with a 12-period deferral schedule, a maintenance contract as deferred revenue (`AEG`), a recurring rent template (`0 0 1 * *`); every month a sale, a purchase, a utilities invoice, a customer collection, a supplier payment, salaries split over the cost centres, a month-end accrual with its automatic reversal, bank charges, a quarterly rent payment and a consultants journal on the ERP project; then the daily routines run (auto-reversals, recurring generation, deferral postings, nothing left waiting), months before the previous one are hard-closed and the previous month soft-closed, `IQT`'s eighth-month purchase is corrected through the manual-journal correction path (reversal and replacement in the first open period, links both ways), and the invariant harness runs on the tenant and must pass before the seed commits (`DemoSeeder.VerifyAsync` reruns it on demand). Amounts are USD figures scaled to the functional currency (IQD ×1310 to the dinar, AED ×3.67 to the fils) so the three sets of books differ. Measured locally: 332 journals, 410 ledger entries, 33 period closings in about 32 s; the reseed test asserts the same counts twice and the same fixed ids. On the way: per-request memo caches in the Organization and Accounting lookups that the engine hits on every line (company, branch, currency, dimension directory, period resolution, active rules), cleared on every save, cut the seed from 59 s; the demo test budget stays one minute per run. |

### M3 slices

| Slice | Status | Notes |
|-------|--------|-------|
| 3.1 Items and UoM | done | Module `Quicker.Items` (+ `.Contracts`, `.TestSupport`, `.Tests`), migration `V0014__items.sql` (13 tenant tables). Items with bilingual names and descriptions, type (stock, non_stock, service, kit, assembly), tracking policy (none, lot, serial, lot and serial; expiry and FEFO need lots), shelf life, list price with currency, weight, volume, HS code, origin, image (an attachment id), custom fields (host `item`), audit trail; categories as a tree with a materialised path (move rewrites the subtree, cycles refused, costing-method and posting-group overrides per category); brands; attributes with values and variants defined by them (SKU unique per tenant, one variant per combination, values in use cannot be removed). **Units** (hard scenario 9): the base unit's row is created with the item at 1/1 and cannot go; every other unit carries an exact rational factor to the base (`numeric(24,12)`, 24/1 for a carton of 24 pieces); sales and purchase defaults; the base unit is locked while other units exist. `ItemUomMath` in the contracts converts quantity × numerator ÷ denominator and back, and refuses anything that is not a whole number at the target unit's precision (`quantity.not_exact_in_base` / `quantity.not_exact_in_uom`) instead of rounding; `IItemDirectory` (memoised per unit of work) is what stock documents will convert through. Barcodes per unit and per variant, unique in the tenant, EAN-13/EAN-8/UPC-A check digits verified, `GET /items/by-barcode/{code}`. Suppliers per item (partner id, their code, unit, lead time, last price, one preferred), settings per company (costing override, standard cost, posting-group override, default warehouse, negative-stock override) and per warehouse (reorder point, min/max, safety stock, lead time, default bin, cycle-count class), substitutes in priority order. Bills of material: kit or assembly matching the item type, versioned with one active version, lines in any unit of the component converted exactly, service items refused, cycles refused (direct and through nested active bills, with the route named), `explode` walks nested bills for a quantity and lists every requirement in base units with the scrap allowance and its route. Paged list with the filter language (`type`, `tracking`, `categoryId`, `brandId`, `isActive`, `hasVariants`, custom fields) and `q` over code and names in any language; CSV export/import with units and barcodes packed into two columns and JSON import, upsert by code, all or nothing with the failing row named. New contracts: `IUomDirectory` (Organization), `IPostingGroupDirectory` (Accounting). Permissions `inventory.item.read/manage`, `inventory.bom.read/manage`; new role template `inventory_manager`. Tests: 6 — the scenario 9 property test (ten thousand random carton/half-carton/dozen/piece movements through the real directory against exact integer arithmetic, zero drift at every step, the eighth of a carton exact, the third refused, and the same over the API), a rational-factor round-trip property test, the item master end to end (categories, attributes, variants, barcodes, suppliers, settings, substitutes, search, audit), CSV/JSON import and export with custom fields and tenant isolation, bills of material — plus the schema suite over the 13 new tables (210 tests in all). |
| 3.2 Warehouses and stock ledger | done | Module `Quicker.Inventory` (+ `.Contracts`, `.TestSupport`, `.Tests`), migration `V0015__inventory_stock.sql` (8 tenant tables), seed `S0006` (the reservation-expiry schedule). **Warehouses** per company (kinds `standard`, `in_transit`, `quarantine`, `consignment`, `virtual`; optional branch; `binsEnabled`; a per-warehouse negative-stock override) and **bins** (code, zone, pick sequence; refused on in-transit warehouses; a bin with stock cannot go). `itm_item_warehouse_settings.warehouse_id` and `itm_item_company_settings.default_warehouse_id` are now real foreign keys and the item settings validate them (`warehouse.not_found`, `item_settings.default_warehouse_invalid`). **Stock ledger** (ADR-0008, quantity side): `IInventoryPosting.PostAsync` is the one way stock moves; a posting is one document movement in one transaction, replayable by idempotency key, and writes `inv_stock_ledger_entries` (global identity sequence, sign checked by entry type: 16 types from `purchase_receipt` to `opening`; append-only) in the item's base unit after the exact conversion of 3.1, with the entered quantity and unit kept. Lines are validated against the engine rules (stock items only, variant required when the item has variants, warehouse of the same company, active warehouse, bin required/forbidden by the warehouse, inventory period open for the actor with `inventory.period.post_in_soft_closed` for soft-closed, warehouse scopes of the principal). **Balances** `inv_stock_balances` keyed by company/item/variant/warehouse/bin/lot/serial (nil uuids for "none"): created with `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, then locked with `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` in a fixed key order (no deadlocks), and only then checked against the negative-stock policy (item setting for the company → warehouse → company `NegativeStockPolicy`), so twenty concurrent shipments of the last unit yield exactly one success and nineteen `409 stock.insufficient` with a `why` naming on hand, reserved and requested. **Reservations** (`IStockReservations`): a document reserves stock (checked against availability = on hand − reserved − quality hold, the reservation reducing availability for everyone else), releases it, lets it expire (daily job `inventory.reservations.expire` at 00:15 UTC across tenants), and consumes it when the same document ships (the engine takes the reservation first, so a reserved unit is never blocked by its own hold). **Availability** (`GET /inventory/stock/availability`): on hand, reserved, quality hold, available, in transit (derived from open transfers into the warehouse). **Two-step transfers** (`inv_transfers`, numbered `TRF-{yyyy}-{seq:5}` per company): draft → `ship` posts `transfer_out` at the source and `transfer_in` into the company's in-transit warehouse (a `transferPairId` links the pair) → `receive` in parts or in full posts the same pair from transit into the destination (`partially_received` → `received`); cancel only while draft; every step audited. **Inquiry**: balances per key, global stock search (`GET /inventory/stock?q=&companyId=&warehouseId=&onlyAvailable=`) paged by item and warehouse, and the ledger newest first by sequence with item, warehouse, date, source document filters. Invariant harness check `stock_balances_match_ledger` (on hand and reserved rebuilt from the ledger and open reservations in both directions). Permissions `inventory.warehouse.read/manage`, `inventory.stock.read`, `inventory.transfer.read/manage/ship/receive`, `inventory.reservation.manage`, `inventory.period.post_in_soft_closed`. Tests: 3 scenarios — scenario 4 (twenty users ship the last unit at once through two-step transfers; then the company allows negative stock and all twenty pass; the ledger holds 20 paired entries summing to zero and the search shows −19/+20), reservations (hold, release, expiry job, consume on ship, partial then full receipt, in transit, cancel) and bins/periods/scopes/engine rules (a warehouse-scoped user is refused elsewhere, soft-closed period refused without the permission, every engine refusal code); each ends with the invariant harness; plus the schema suite over the 8 tables and the Items suite now creating a real warehouse (213 tests in all). |
| 3.3 Costing engine | done | Migration `V0016__inventory_costing.sql` (6 tenant tables; `remaining_quantity` and `costed_at_expected` leave the append-only stock ledger and are derived; `entered_unit_cost` and `applies_to_sle_id` join it as the valuation's inputs). **Value entries** `inv_stock_value_entries` (append-only: `direct_cost`, `indirect_cost`, `expected_cost`, `expected_cost_reversal`, `revaluation`, `variance`, `cost_adjustment`; the GL date and the movement's valuation date; the account role, the offset role and its subledger item; the journal entry; the entry it adjusts; the run; a `reason` naming the trigger document) are the only cost figures in the system: a movement's cost is the sum of its rows. **Methods** per company with category and item-per-company overrides: FIFO applies an outbound entry to the oldest inbound layers (`inv_item_applications`, superseded rather than deleted on re-application; the last unit of a layer takes its whole remaining value, so an emptied layer is worth zero), the daily average values a day's outbound entries at the average of the opening balance and that day's receipts (order-independent by construction), standard cost values inbound entries at the standard in force (`inv_standard_cost_versions` with effective dates, the 3.1 company setting as fallback) and posts the difference as a `variance` to the purchase price / assembly / adjustment variance account. **Backdating and re-application** (hard scenarios 1, 2): a value-affecting event at an earlier date (a backdated movement, a late invoice, a landed cost, a new standard) locks the cost scope (`inv_item_cost_scopes`, `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`), re-applies from that date forward and writes `cost_adjustment` rows with the delta and the trigger; transfers carry cost downstream and re-cost their destination scope; FIFO shortfalls (negative stock, a shipment dated before its receipt) are valued at the expected cost, flagged, and applied forward when a later layer covers them. Runs are logged in `inv_cost_adjustment_runs` (trigger, from date, entries walked and re-applied, value entries created, journals posted, amount adjusted) and audited; a walk of more than `Quicker:Inventory:RecostThreshold` entries (2,000) is queued as job `inventory.costing.recost` with the scope flagged `valuationPending` until it completes. **Books**: every value entry posts through the accounting engine in the same unit of work (`SourceModule` `inventory`), Dr/Cr Inventory (or InventoryInTransit for an in-transit warehouse) with the item as INV subledger item against the movement's offset (GRNI with the receipt, COGS, InventoryAdjustment, Scrap, CountVariance, OpeningBalanceEquity, LandedCostClearing, InventoryWriteDown for revaluations), one journal per GL date and walk, and the adjustment's GL date is the movement's date when its inventory period takes postings from the actor, else the first open period's start (ADR-0008 §5). **Late invoices and landed costs** (`IInventoryCosting.AdjustInboundCostAsync`, `POST /inventory/costing/inbound-adjustments`): an invoice reverses the expected cost and posts the actual one (under standard cost, the difference is a variance), a landed cost adds an `indirect_cost` row, and everything the layer fed is re-applied; idempotent by key. **Inquiry**: item cost per scope at a date (`GET /inventory/costing/item-cost`), the valuation report at a date per item and warehouse (`GET /inventory/costing/valuation`: quantity, actual, expected, value; equals the inventory accounts at that date), "why did this cost change" for one entry (`GET /inventory/costing/entries/{sleId}`: value entries in order with reasons, layers consumed or consumers, the runs), runs (`/inventory/costing/runs`), standard cost versions. Daily cost buckets `inv_item_costs` cache the running quantity, value, average, last and standard cost per scope and day. Invariant `inventory_matches_gl`: per company and item, Σ value entries on the inventory accounts = Σ INV subledger journal lines, both ways. Permissions `inventory.costing.read/manage`. Tests: 6 — FIFO layers, a late invoice and a landed cost adjusting COGS with the trigger named, an exact-cost purchase return, a two-step transfer carrying cost through InventoryInTransit; daily average with a backdated receipt re-costing the day, standard cost with variances and a revaluation on a new standard; the **property test** of the roadmap (a month of random receipts and shipments posted in date order and in a random permutation with negative stock allowed yield the same cost per movement and the same valuation, for FIFO and average, and Σ value entries equals the INV journal lines at seven sampled dates); the threshold path (a backdated receipt behind 62 shipments queues the run, the item shows `valuationPending`, the worker completes it and only the five affected shipments carry adjustments). Every scenario ends with the harness. 219 tests in all. |
| 3.4 Adjustments, transfers, assemblies | done | Migration `V0017__inventory_documents.sql` (7 tenant tables; transfers gain `kind` and shortages; the stock ledger gains `offset_role_override`, value entries `offset_posting_group_id`). **Reason codes** (`/inventory/reason-codes`): code, bilingual name, what they apply to (adjustment, count, return, scrap, write_off, shortage), an optional account role override limited to the inventory expense accounts (InventoryAdjustment, CountVariance, Scrap, InventoryWriteDown, Cogs), `requiresNote`. **Adjustment documents** (`/inventory/adjustments`, numbered `ADJ-{yyyy}-{seq:5}` at posting): positive, negative, scrap or opening, one warehouse, lines with item, quantity in any unit, bin, an entered unit cost (positive and opening only; a positive line without one takes the current cost) and a reason code that must apply to the kind (with a note when the reason demands it); draft → submit → posted, or draft → submit → pending_approval → approve (posted) / reject (rejected, editable again) when the company setting `inventory.adjustments.approval` is `required` (the submitter cannot approve; `inventory.adjustment.approve`); cancel while unposted; custom fields (host `stock_adjustment`); posted through the stock and costing engines with the reason's account override honoured by the journal. **Revaluations** (`/inventory/revaluations`, `RVL-{yyyy}-{seq:5}`): an NRV write-down (IAS 2) or a manual revaluation of the stock on hand per item (and warehouse when the company costs per warehouse) at a date to a new unit cost; posting computes the quantity on hand and the current unit cost, writes `revaluation` value entries (Inventory against InventoryWriteDown), under FIFO per open layer (each layer brought to the new unit value, never above its own original cost on a write-down reversal), otherwise on the pool (the reversal capped at the pre-write-down average still attached to the units on hand), and re-applies later movements so issues carry the new value. **Transfers**: `kind` `one_step` lands straight in the destination on ship (received at once, nothing in transit) or `two_step` through transit; on receipt a line may declare a `shortage` with a shortage reason code (and note): it is written off from the transit warehouse as a negative adjustment to the reason's account, the transfer completes when received + shortage = shipped, and the acceptance criterion holds: the in-transit value entries equal the InventoryInTransit account at every date. **Assembly builds** (`/inventory/assemblies`, `ASM-{yyyy}-{seq:5}`): an assembly item, an output quantity in any of its units, a warehouse and either explicit component lines or, by default, the direct components of the item's active assembly bill (new contract `IBomDirectory.BuildAsync`: base units with the scrap allowance; sub-assemblies are built separately); posting consumes the components and produces the assembly in one stock posting, the costing engine values the output at what the components cost (components offset the assembly item's stock, so the books show Dr Inventory(assembly) / Cr Inventory(components)), and a later change to a component's cost (a backdated receipt) re-costs the consumption and cascades to the assembly. Permissions `inventory.reason_code.manage`, `inventory.adjustment.read/manage/approve/post`, `inventory.assembly.read/manage/post`. Tests: 3 scenarios — adjustments end to end (opening at a cost, positive at the current cost, validation of reasons/notes/costs, the approval round trip with a second user, rejection, reason overrides landing on Scrap and InventoryWriteDown); NRV write-down and its capped recovery with the shipment in between carrying the written-down value, a one-step transfer, a two-step transfer with a shortage written off from transit and the in-transit balance equal to the account at both dates; assemblies from the bill with scrap, the output cost roll-up, the journal per item, and the cascade after a backdated component receipt under the daily average. 222 tests in all. |
| 3.5 Lots and serials | done | Migration `V0018__inventory_tracking.sql` (`inv_lots`, `inv_serials`, `inv_serial_events`; the stock ledger references its lot and serial and carries the document's `partner_id` for traceability; document lines carry `lot_number`, `expires_on`, `serial_numbers`), seed `S0007` (the daily lot-expiry schedule). **Tracking in the engine** (`TrackingResolver`): a lot-tracked item moves by lot — a receipt names or creates the lot by number (expiry from the line or the item's shelf life; required when the item says so; the supplier kept from the receipt's partner), an issue names the lot it takes and is refused with the FEFO plan in `why.suggestion` when it does not (`stock.lot_required`); quarantined, recalled or expired lots cannot be shipped, transferred or consumed (`stock.lot_blocked`, `stock.lot_expired`; scrap, adjustments and returns still move them). A serialised item moves one serial at a time: whole units, one number per unit (`stock.serial_count`), unique per item, created on receipt, refused when already on hand (`stock.serial_in_stock`, a transfer's inbound half excepted), refused on the way out when unknown, not on hand, in another warehouse or in repair; the line splits into one ledger entry per serial (each with its own balance row, cost and history). After a posting, serials get their status (in_stock, in_transit, returned, sold, returned_to_supplier, scrapped, consumed, consigned), position and counterparty, and an event per movement; the stock of a blocked lot is kept on quality hold on every balance row. **Lots** (`/inventory/lots`): list (item, status, expiring before, search), create ahead of receipt, edit dates and supplier lot, custom fields (host `lot`), FEFO suggestion (`/lots/suggest`), status changes (`inventory.lot.manage`): quarantine, recall (its reference required; answers with the impact: stock on hand and on hold per warehouse, the customers who received the lot with quantities, the receipts it came from), expire, release; a hold releases the lot's open reservations; the daily job `inventory.lots.expire` (00:20 UTC, all tenants) expires lots past their date. Backward and forward traceability (`/lots/{id}/trace`): inbound and outbound movements with documents, counterparties and serials, balances by warehouse, the serials of the lot (hard scenario 12). **Serials** (`/inventory/serials`): list (item, status, warehouse, lot, search), by number, status changes into repair and back (`inventory.serial.manage`; a unit in repair is on hold and cannot ship), and the history (`/serials/{id}/history`): every movement with its document, quantity, cost and counterparty and every status change, oldest first, one query (hard scenario 13). Balances and the ledger show lot numbers, expiry, lot status, serial numbers and the partner. Documents: adjustment and assembly lines carry `lotNumber`, `expiresOn`, `serialNumbers` (the output of an assembly too); transfer lines carry `lotId`/`lotNumber` and `serialNumbers`, and a receipt may name which serials arrived and which are short. Tests: 2 scenarios — lots (expiry required, shelf life, FEFO plan, expired refused, reservation released by the recall, hold on the stock and on a return of the recalled lot, customers listed, release) and serials (receipt split per serial, uniqueness, sold → returned → repaired → transferred through transit → resold, the timeline of ten events with costs and notes, balances per serial). 224 tests in all. |
| 3.6 Counts | done | Migration `V0019__inventory_counts.sql` (`inv_counts`, `inv_count_snapshots` append-only, `inv_count_lines`). A **count** (`/inventory/counts`) is planned for one warehouse with a scope (full, cycle by the items' cycle-count classes through the new `IItemDirectory.ItemsForCycleCountAsync`, bins, items), a count date, `blind` (expected quantities hidden on the sheet until the review) and `blockMovements`. **Freeze** locks the warehouse's balance rows, snapshots the expected quantity per item, variant, bin, lot and serial and the global ledger sequence at that instant, numbers the count (`CNT-{yyyy}-{seq:5}`) and opens the sheet (one line per key with stock). **Counting**: entries by line or by key (item, bin, lot number, serial number), a serial counted present or missing, stock found off the sheet becomes a line with nothing expected, recounts keep the previous count for the record and hold the review. **Review** computes every line: `movement_since_freeze` = Σ ledger quantity for the key with sequence after the freeze and posting date on or before the count date (the count's own entries excluded), `variance = counted − (expected + movement)`, valued at the item's average (else expected) cost; the warehouse keeps working the whole time (hard scenario 11). **Approval** (`inventory.count.approve`) re-reads the movements and requires a count reason code (with a note where the reason demands it) on every variance; **posting** (`inventory.count.post`) re-reads them once more and posts the variances as signed `count_variance` movements (bin, lot, serial; a missing serial is scrapped, a found one received) with the reason's account override, through the stock and costing engines, one stock posting and journal per count. A count with `blockMovements` refuses every movement in its warehouse (or, for a bins scope, in its bins) with `stock.warehouse_counting` until it is posted or cancelled. Permissions `inventory.count.read/manage/enter/approve/post`. Test: 1 scenario — a blind full count of a binned warehouse with a lot-tracked and a serialised item; a shipment and a receipt posted after the freeze and one after the count date; counts entered, a serial marked missing, jam found in the wrong bin, a recount; the review shows the movements and variances (−1 tea, +3 jam, −1 lamp) against snapshot plus movements; reasons, approval, posting; balances, the scrapped serial and the count's ledger entries checked; the invariant harness; then a bins-scoped blocking count that stops movements in one bin only, and a cycle count by class. 225 tests in all. |
| 3.7 Replenishment | done | Migration `V0020__inventory_replenishment.sql` (`inv_replenishment_runs`, `inv_replenishment_suggestions` with one open suggestion per item and warehouse), seed `S0008` (the daily planner schedule). The **planner** (`POST /inventory/replenishment/run` for a company or one warehouse, and the platform job `inventory.replenishment.plan` at 01:00 UTC across every active tenant and company) reads the planning parameters of every active item in each stock warehouse through the new `IItemDirectory.PlanningParametersAsync` (reorder point, min, max, safety stock, lead time, the preferred supplier and its lead time from the 3.1 settings), projects the stock as available (on hand − reserved − quality hold) + transfers in transit to the warehouse + the purchasing module's incoming supply (new contract `IIncomingSupply`, a no-op until M4), and when the projection is at or below the reorder point (or below the minimum, or below the maximum when nothing else is set) suggests buying up to the maximum — or, without one, back to reorder point + safety stock, never leaving the projection under the minimum — needed by the run date plus the lead time, from the preferred supplier. Every suggestion carries its **explanation**: as-of date, on hand, reserved, hold, available, in transit, on order, projected, the parameters, the trigger, the target and the formula. A later run refreshes the open suggestion rather than adding another and closes it as `superseded` when the need is gone; suggestions are **accepted** (with the quantity and supplier to buy; the purchase order lands with M4) or **dismissed** with a reason; runs are listed with what they checked, created, refreshed and closed. Permissions `inventory.replenishment.read/manage`. Test: 1 scenario — parameters and a preferred supplier, a first run explaining 180 to buy up to the max with the reservation counted, a transfer in transit refreshing the suggestion instead of duplicating it, a reorder-point-plus-safety-stock case without a max, dismissal with a reason, acceptance with an override quantity, the need disappearing after a receipt (superseded), the run log, and the platform job planning the tenant; the harness at the end. 226 tests in all. |
| 3.8 Mobile scanning v1 | done | A **PWA route group** at `/m` in the web app (`apps/web/src/routes/mobile`): its own thumb-first shell (network badge, language toggle, tab bar: home, count, transfer, queue) on the same session and permissions, installable through `vite-plugin-pwa` (manifest opening on `/m`, app shell precached, fonts cached on first use, API responses never cached). **Scan box**: one input for hardware scanners, keyboards and the camera; the camera decodes with the browser's `BarcodeDetector` where it exists and the ZXing decoder (`@zxing/browser`, loaded on demand) elsewhere. **Codes resolve** by barcode (`GET /items/by-barcode`) then item code and are cached on the device, so labels seen once resolve offline; bins match the warehouse's bin list. **Count flow**: choose the frozen count, scan the bin (when the warehouse has bins), scan the item, type the quantity (lot and serial prompts by the item's tracking); each capture sets the counted quantity of that line through `POST /inventory/counts/{id}/entries`. **Transfer flow**: scan lines from the operator's warehouse to another, shipped as a one-step transfer on sync. **Offline queue** (`localStorage`): every capture is queued first and replayed in order when online (on capture, when the network returns, or on demand); an API rejection keeps the capture with its problem for the operator to fix or drop; a network failure stops the run and waits. Desktop navigation gains the "Scanner" entry (`inventory.count.enter`). Found and fixed on the way: `GET /inventory/warehouses/{id}/bins` answered 500 (a string comparer in the ordering that the database cannot translate); the ledger test now lists bins. Test: Playwright `mobile` project (Pixel 7 emulation, `e2e/mobile-scanning.spec.ts`): sign-up, set-up through the API, bin then barcode then quantity synced to the count sheet, a second capture queued offline and replayed by itself when the network returns, the queue screen, then Arabic (RTL), with axe on every screen. |
| 3.9 Demo seed v3 | done | `DemoStock` extends the same `demo` tenant after the books, through the modules' services inside the seeder's unit of work: a category tree (6 roots, 22 leaves), 16 brands, size and colour attributes, three item posting groups, seven reason codes; **5,000 items** over 22 product families (food, home, electronics, apparel, pharmacy, industrial) with bilingual names, EAN-13 barcodes (a carton unit with its own barcode on a third of them), list prices, countries of origin, preferred suppliers with lead times on 40 %, FEFO lot tracking with shelf life on fresh food and pharmacy, serials on electronics, lot-and-serial on medical devices, size × colour **variants** on half the apparel; **eleven warehouses** (eight stocked: four for the trading company including a cold store, two for the importer, two in Dubai, three of them with 48 bins each; plus one in-transit per company); planning parameters and cycle-count classes on a quarter of the stocked items; **opening stock** posted through the stock posting engine in batches of 250 lines per warehouse (quantities, bins, lots with expiry, serial numbers, unit costs in the functional currency from a USD draw), so every unit is costed (the importer runs FIFO, the others average) and booked, and the invariant harness runs again before the commit. Everything derives from the item's ordinal (`DemoIds.Draw`), so a reseed reproduces the same dataset. Found and fixed on the way: the costing engine's drain guard counted distinct scopes rather than re-walks of one scope, so any document with more than 200 distinct items failed with `costing.reapplication_diverged`; the guard now bounds the walks of one scope (50) and a costing test posts a 210-line receipt. Migration `V0021__statistics_refresh.sql` adds `app.refresh_statistics()` (owner-defined, callable by the application role): a bulk load inside one transaction otherwise leaves the planner believing the tables are empty, and the costing walks and the harness were planned as if for a handful of rows (the harness alone took a minute); the seed calls it after the items and before the harness. Numbers today: 5,000 items, 1,665 variants, 8,223 opening entries, 3,194 lots, 1,138 serials; the seed takes about six minutes on a laptop (ASSUMPTIONS A-104; the demo test's budget is ten, the CI job's timeout 45). The demo test asserts 5,000 items, variants, lots, serials, eight stocked warehouses, 144 bins, a value entry for every opening entry, INV journal lines, reason codes, planning rows, the harness green, and that a reseed reproduces the same counts. |
| M3 web pass | done | Ten screens under `/inventory/*` (EN/AR, RTL, keyboard shortcuts `g i/b/k/x/v/y/z/q/g/9`), each on the company the accounting screens remember, with a warehouse filter beside it: **Items** (search with keyset paging; create/edit with type, base unit, tracking, expiry and FEFO, category and brand, list price; detail with units, barcodes, variants and suppliers; categories and brands managed in place), **Warehouses** (kinds, negative-stock policy, bins in pick order), **Stock** (by item with an availability search, balances by bin/lot/serial, the ledger with a date range), **Adjustments** (positive/negative/scrap/opening lines with reason codes, unit costs, bins, lots and serials; submit → approval when configured → posted; reason codes managed in place), **Transfers** (one- or two-step, ship and receive), **Assemblies** (output and components, post), **Lots & serials** (filters, status changes with reason and recall reference, the lot trace with on-hand by warehouse, movements and customers shipped to; the serial timeline), **Counts** (plan, freeze, enter, recount, review with a reason per variance line, approve, post), **Replenishment** (run the planner, suggestions with their explanation, accept/dismiss, the run log), **Valuation** (by date and warehouse with totals; the cost adjustment runs with re-run). Found and fixed on the way: the contract published the item categories with the account categories' schema (two types named `CategorySummary`/`SaveCategoryRequest` shared one schema id, so the typed client had the wrong shape); the item types are renamed, a schema-id collision now fails the document (`SchemaReferenceIds`), and the contract check accepts recorded corrections (`contracts/contract-corrections.json`; ADR-0012 amended). Test: Playwright `e2e/inventory-journeys.spec.ts` (company and chart, warehouses with a bin, an item, reason codes, a positive adjustment posted, stock, a one-step transfer received, a count with a reasoned variance approved and posted, the valuation equal to 98 × 250, a planner run, then Arabic right-to-left), axe on every screen. |

### M4 slices

| Slice | Status | Notes |
|-------|--------|-------|
| 4.0 Workflow core | done | Module `Quicker.Workflow` (+ `.Contracts`, `.Tests`), migration `V0022__workflow.sql` (8 tenant tables, `inv_adjustments.approval_request_id`), schedule `workflow.escalation` (S0009, hourly). **Definitions** per entity type and trigger (`on_submit`, `on_post_attempt`, `on_block` + block kind), versioned in a lineage: a draft is edited in place, editing an active one yields the next draft version, activating retires the version active for the same key. **Rules** in the safe expression grammar (`Expressions`: lexer, parser, type checker, evaluator; fields, comparisons incl. `=`/`<>`, arithmetic, `in` with `[..]` or `(..)`, `and/or/not`, functions `amount_in('USD')`, `days_since`, `days_until`, `today`, `lower`, `upper`, `contains`, `starts_with`, `len`, `abs`, `round`, `is_empty`), parsed and type-checked at save time against the **catalogue** each module registers (`IWorkflowSubjectProvider`: fields with types and bilingual labels, block kinds, `LoadAsync`, `OnDecidedAsync`); `amount_in` converts through the company's spot rate of the day. **Steps**: named members or every active holder of a role (scoped to the document's company unless told otherwise; `IRoleDirectory.MembersInRoleAsync`), mode any/all/quorum, timeout and escalation target, delegate/comment flags; the requester is never an approver (`workflow.no_approvers` when nobody else is left). **Requests** stamp the definition version, the matched rule and the evaluation (every rule, its condition, whether it held, the values), open steps in order, resolve approvers when a step opens, and end approved, rejected, cancelled or auto-approved (recorded when no rule holds). **Actions** (append-only `wf_actions` + audit events + notifications through Collaboration): approve, reject, request changes, delegate, comment, cancel, escalate; self-approval refused; a comment is required to reject; the module's `OnDecidedAsync` runs in the approver's transaction and its failure fails the decision (a posting the books refuse rolls the approval back). **Blocks**: `RaiseBlockAsync(kind, entity, why)` records the block and routes it to the on_block definition of its kind (rules evaluate the block's own values, typed when raised); an approval grants a single-use **override** (who, why, expires after the definition's hours, default 168) that the module consumes once through `ConsumeOverrideAsync`; no definition leaves the block open. **Delegations** (member → member, all definitions or one, dated) let the stand-in see the request and act on the principal's behalf (recorded as such). **Escalation** job (`workflow.escalate`, hourly over every tenant): an overdue step gains its escalation target's members, restarts its SLA and notifies; without a target the approvers are reminded once. **Stock adjustments** are the first real subject: with an active definition, submit runs it (auto-approved posts at once, a match leaves the adjustment `pending_approval` with its request id; the inventory approve/reject endpoints defer to the workflow), an approval posts under the approver's name without needing the post permission, a rejection returns the adjustment with the reason, cancelling withdraws the request.
