API `/api/v1/workflow/*` (catalogue, expression validation, definitions, inbox and actions, blocks, overrides, delegations). **Web**: `/approvals` (inbox, all requests for readers, delegations; detail with the why panel, values, steps, history, block and override, approve/reject/request changes/delegate/comment/withdraw) and `/workflows` (definitions grid; editor driven by the catalogue with rules checked against the grammar as typed, steps with roles or members, escalation, activate/retire). Tests (`Quicker.Workflow.Tests`, 5): catalogue and grammar (valid, unknown field with position, non-boolean, type clash, definition validation codes, versions); the acceptance rule `amount_in('USD') > 10000` routed to the finance role with self-approval refused, a rejection with its reason, a decision the module refuses leaving the request pending, withdrawal; blocks routed to their definition, override recorded with who and why and consumed exactly once, unrouted block left open; delegation acting on behalf, explicit hand-over, escalation by the job with the SLA restarted; the real stock adjustment path end to end; the invariant harness after each. |

| 4.1 Supplier master | done | Module `Quicker.Partners` (+ `.Contracts`, `.TestSupport`, `.Tests`), migration `V0023__partners.sql` (11 tenant tables). **Partners** (DOMAIN_MODEL §7): one tenant-wide record per legal person with bilingual legal and trade names, kind (organisation, person), the customer/supplier/employee roles, an intercompany link to a group company, a parent partner, contact details, notes, custom fields (host `partner`), audit trail; codes unique per tenant, upper-cased; lists page by keyset and search by code, name in either language or email. **Contacts** (one primary per partner), **addresses** (billing, shipping, legal, other; structured bilingual content; one default per use), **bank accounts** (IBAN checked by mod-97, account number and IBAN stored only AES-GCM-encrypted under the platform key with masked forms for display; `POST .../reveal` needs `partners.supplier.reveal_bank_account` and writes an audit event naming who revealed what), **tax registrations** (VAT, TIN, CRN, other; unique per partner, country, type and number; validity dates). **Supplier accounts** per company: supplier group, payment terms, delivery terms, supplier posting group (kind checked), tax group (M5), withholding code, currency (defaults to the company's), lead time, price and over-receipt tolerances, requires-PO, hold (`purchase`, `payment`, `all` with a reason, who and when; released explicitly), active flag; blanks default from the group and the **effective** terms are what documents read. **Configuration**: supplier groups (posting group, default payment and delivery terms), payment terms (due from the invoice date, the end of its month or the delivery; due days; optional instalments whose percentages sum to 100; early-payment discount; business-days-only), delivery terms (Incoterms EXW, FCA, FOB, CFR, CIF, DAP, DDP seeded per tenant as system rows), withholding tax codes (rate, withheld at invoice or payment, optional threshold in a currency); IMMEDIATE, NET15, NET30, NET60 and EOM30 seeded per tenant. **Contract** `IPartnerDirectory`: find by id or code, the effective supplier terms, `EnsureSupplierAsync(company, partner, purpose)` refusing unregistered (`supplier.not_registered`), inactive (`supplier.inactive`) and held (`supplier.on_hold` with the reason) suppliers per purpose, withholding codes, and `ScheduleAsync` (due dates through the company's working-day calendar when the terms say so, amounts allocated by `RoundingPolicy` so the instalments sum to the whole in the currency's minor unit). API `/api/v1/partners/*`. Role templates: purchaser and accountant gain `partners.terms.manage`. **Web**: `/purchasing/suppliers` (company's supplier accounts with effective terms and holds; new supplier = partner + account in one step; detail tabs for the account with hold/release, contacts, addresses, bank accounts masked with reveal, tax registrations) and `/purchasing/settings` (payment terms with instalments and a due-date preview, delivery terms, supplier groups, withholding codes), EN/AR, with a Playwright journey. Tests (`Quicker.Partners.Tests`, 3): the partner with everything attached (validation codes, one primary contact, one default address per use, invalid IBAN refused, encrypted at rest and masked, reveal forbidden without the permission and audited with it, duplicate registration refused, search in Arabic and by email); supplier accounts (posting-group kind, tolerances, group defaults and overrides, the directory's effective terms, holds per purpose, release, role removal refused while accounts are active, inactive refused); payment schedules (50/50 of 100.01 = 50.01 + 50.00, end of month, business days moving a Friday to Sunday, delivery basis, early-discount window) and withholding codes. |
| 4.2 Requisition to purchase order | done | Module `Quicker.Purchasing` (+ `.Contracts`, `.TestSupport`, `.Tests`), migration `V0024__purchasing.sql` (13 tenant tables: requisitions and lines, RFQs with lines, invited suppliers, supplier quotes and quote lines, blanket agreements and lines, purchase orders and lines, order revisions (append-only), budget commitments). **Requisitions** (`REQ-{yyyy}-{seq}`): lines by item code with quantity in any of the item's units (converted to base), an estimate, a warehouse, a dimension set and a suggested supplier; submitted through the workflow engine as `purchase_requisition` (fields amount, currency, lineCount, neededBy, department, hasWarehouse); no active definition approves at once; `POST .../orders` turns the approved lines into one purchase-order draft per suggested (or named) supplier, marks the lines ordered and the requisition `ordered` when nothing is left; cancelling an order gives its requisition lines back. **RFQs** (`RFQ-{yyyy}-{seq}`): lines, suppliers invited (each must be a registered, unheld supplier), sent by email to every invited supplier (`rfq.supplier_no_email` names the one without an address; addresses can be given per supplier at send), quotes recorded by the buyer per supplier in the supplier's currency with lead time, freight and other charges, declines, `POST .../compare` ranking complete quotes first, then by landed total converted to the company's currency at today's spot rate, then by lead time (the ranking is kept on each quote and shown), `POST .../award` closing the request as awarded and creating a purchase-order draft priced as quoted with expected dates from the lead time. **Blanket agreements** (`BPA-{yyyy}-{seq}`): a supplier, a validity, a currency, an optional committed amount and lines with agreed quantity and price; activated, closed or cancelled; orders that name an agreement line release against it at approval (`agreement.over_release`, `agreement.over_committed`, `agreement.not_active`, `agreement.uom_mismatch` refused at submission) and give the release back when cancelled. **Purchase orders** (`PO-{yyyy}-{seq}`): drafted in the supplier's currency (default from the supplier account, spot rate to the company's currency on the order date), payment and delivery terms from the effective supplier terms, lines priced with a discount, per-line expected date and warehouse, custom fields (host `purchase_order`); `submit` checks the supplier (`supplier.on_hold` with the reason, `supplier.not_registered`) and the releases, then runs the workflow as `purchase_order` (fields amount, currency, amountRc, supplierCode, lineCount, warehouseCode, orderDate, revision, hasAgreement, fromRequisition; block kind `budget_exceeded` reserved for M6) so "an order above threshold routes to approval" is one `amount_in('IQD') > 10000` rule; approval records **commitments** per line (role `Inventory` for stock items, `Expense` otherwise; the month of the expected date; amounts in the order and company currencies), rejection keeps the order editable with the reason; **change orders** snapshot the current version into `pur_order_revisions`, undo the approval (commitments released, releases given back), re-apply and re-submit, so revision 2 goes through approval again and the email subject names the revision; refused once anything was received; **send** emails the supplier a bilingual HTML order (Scriban template, embedded) plus a text body and marks it sent; cancel and close. **Contracts**: `IPurchaseOrderDirectory` (open lines per company, supplier or order; a line by id, for receipts in 4.3) and `IIncomingSupply` implemented here (approved, sent and partly received lines with their remaining base quantity feed the replenishment planner of 3.7; the inventory module's empty default is replaced at host registration). Permissions `purchasing.requisition|rfq|agreement|order.read|manage` and `purchasing.order.send`; the purchaser template gets them all. API `/api/v1/purchasing/{requisitions,rfqs,agreements,orders}`. **Web**: `/purchasing/requisitions`, `/purchasing/rfqs` (invite, send, record quotes, compare with ranks, award), `/purchasing/orders` (submit, send with an optional address and message, change order with a reason, cancel, close; tabs for lines, revisions and commitments), `/purchasing/agreements`, EN/AR, with a Playwright journey. Tests (`Quicker.Purchasing.Tests`, 4, each ending with the invariant harness where books are touched): a requisition approved at once and ordered per supplier, the approved order's commitment and its open line as incoming supply, cancelling giving lines and commitments back; an order above the threshold pending finance, approved with commitments, changed into revision 2 and approved again, sent by email (HTML in both languages, the message included), another rejected with the reason and edited; an RFQ sent to four suppliers (one without an address refused, then given one), three quotes in two currencies compared at the spot rate (13,650 = 13,650 tie broken by lead time, then 14,000), the winner awarded into a USD order; a blanket agreement activated, released 60 of 100, 50 more refused with the numbers, the release given back on cancel, a supplier on purchase hold refused with the reason and accepted after release. |
| 4.3 Goods receipts | done | Module `Quicker.Purchasing`, migration `V0025__purchasing_receipts.sql` (`pur_receipts`, `pur_receipt_lines`). A **goods receipt** (`GRN-{yyyy}-{seq}`) is drafted against one approved, sent or partly received order: lines name the order line, a quantity in any of the item's units, an optional bin, lot number, expiry and serial numbers; the receipt names the warehouse (the order's by default), the posting date, the supplier's delivery note, notes and custom fields (host `purchase_receipt`). Each line is checked against the order as it stands: the order line must be open, the item a stock item (services and expense items are received on the invoice, `receipt.non_stock_line`), and the received total per line may not exceed the ordered quantity plus the supplier account's over-receipt tolerance (`receipt.over_receipt` with ordered, received, requested, tolerance and the maximum). `GET .../receipts/receivable` lists the open lines with what is left and what the tolerance still allows. **Posting** goes through the stock engine (`IInventoryPosting`, entry type `purchase_receipt`) with the expected unit cost = the order price after its line discount, converted at the receipt-date spot rate to the company's currency, `CostIsExpected`, lots/serials/bins passed through so the engine enforces the item's tracking (`stock.lot_required`, expiry, whole serial units); the costing engine books Dr Inventory / Cr GRNI with the receipt as the GRNI subledger item, and the receipt keeps each line's booked amount, its ledger entry and the journal. The order's lines gain the received quantity in the order unit and become `partially_received` or `received`; the order follows. **Reversal** (posted receipts only, with a reason, on or after the posting date, refused once a line is invoiced or returned) posts a `purchase_return` applied to each original entry, so stock leaves at exactly what came in and GRNI nets to zero; the order's received quantities are given back. Drafts are edited and deleted. **Invariant** `grni_matches_receipts` in the harness: per company and receipt, Σ (credit − debit) of the GRNI-subledger journal lines equals Σ (expected − invoiced − returned) of the receipt's lines, zero for a reversed receipt, in both directions. **Contract** `IPurchaseReceiptDirectory` (open lines with what is still uninvoiced; a line by id) for invoices (4.4), landed costs (4.5) and returns (4.6). Permissions `purchasing.receipt.read|manage|post`; the purchaser and warehouse-operator templates get them. API `/api/v1/purchasing/receipts`. **Web**: `/purchasing/receipts` (open order lines pre-filled with what is left, lot/expiry/serial fields where the item is tracked, post, reverse with a reason, delete drafts) and **mobile receiving** at `/m/receive` (the order's open lines, a scan adds one to the matching line, quantities typed, posted at once into the operator's warehouse), EN/AR, both covered by Playwright. Tests (`Quicker.Purchasing.Tests`, +1): a USD order received in two parts within a 10 % tolerance (a third refused with the numbers), the lot demanded at posting, expected cost 1.80 × 1300 per unit, GRNI equal to the receipt value per receipt, stock on hand, the order's line and header statuses, the change order refused after a receipt, the directory's uninvoiced lines, reversal (reason and date validated) netting GRNI to zero and giving the order line back, drafts deleted, the harness green with the new check counting both receipts. |
| 4.4 Supplier invoices | done | Module `Quicker.Purchasing`, migration `V0026__purchasing_invoices.sql` (`pur_invoices`, `pur_invoice_lines`, `pur_match_results`, `ap_open_items`; `pur_receipt_lines.sle_ids`). A **supplier invoice** (`PI-{yyyy}-{seq}`; kind `invoice` or `expense`) carries lines of three kinds: against a posted **receipt line** (three-way match; the item, unit and expected price come from the receipt), against an **order line** of a service or expense item that has no receipt (two-way match), or a free **expense line** on an account role (default `PurchaseExpense`, a new role with template account 6155; control roles refused). Currency defaults to the supplier account's; matched lines must be in the order's currency; the rate to the company's currency is the spot rate of the posting date. Totals: net, tax (0 until the tax engine, M5), **withholding at invoice** from the supplier's WHT code (rate, threshold in the same currency, allocated to the lines with `RoundingPolicy`) and the payable; the due date and instalments come from the supplier's payment terms through `IPartnerDirectory.ScheduleAsync`. **Matching** on submit against the supplier account's effective tolerances: price variance per line (invoice price after discount against the expected price, per cent), quantity variance against what is still uninvoiced (plus the quantity tolerance of the ordered quantity), and a **duplicate check** on the supplier's reference (case-insensitive, among live invoices of the same supplier); the result (`matched`, `price_variance`, `qty_variance`, `duplicate_suspect`) is kept with per-line details. A breach **blocks** the invoice: a workflow block of that kind is raised (`purchase_invoice` block kinds `price_variance`, `qty_variance`, `duplicate_suspect`); with no on-block definition it stays open and the invoice says so; with one, the approver's decision grants an override, and the next submission consumes it and records it on the match. The engine now routes an open block again when a definition is activated after the block was raised. Then the on-submit definition decides (fields amount, currency, amountRc, supplierCode, kind, matchStatus, hasVariance, hasOverride, lineCount) or the invoice is approved at once. **Posting** (approved invoices): for each receipt line the costing engine's inbound adjustment (`invoice`) settles every stock entry of the line at the invoice price × rate (the expected cost reversed, the actual cost posted, quantities already sold re-applied through COGS, standard-cost items through PPV), the receipt line keeping the value the engine booked; the journal in the invoice currency at the invoice rate: Dr GRNI per receipt (subledger item the receipt, keys the item posting group and warehouse), Dr `PurchaseExpense` (or the line's role) for service and expense lines, Cr `WhtPayable`, Cr `AP` (subledger item the invoice, the supplier posting group, the due date); the receipt lines gain the invoiced quantity and value (allocated from the GRNI lines actually booked, so the GRNI invariant holds to the minor unit), the order lines their invoiced quantity, the order's **commitments** are consumed, and one **AP open item** per instalment is created (original and remaining in the invoice currency and the company's, the booked rate, discount date and rate). **Reversal** (posted, nothing settled) mirrors the journal through the posting engine's reversal, prices the receipt entries back to their expected cost, gives quantities, commitments and receipt values back and closes the open items. `GET .../invoices/invoicable` lists what a supplier can still invoice; `GET .../invoices/open-items` the payables (the payables module of 4.7 builds on them). Permissions `purchasing.invoice.read|manage|post` (the accountant and payables-clerk templates already carry them). **Web**: `/purchasing/invoices` (supplier, kind, reference, date, currency, WHT switch; the uninvoiced receipt and service lines picked from a list, expense lines typed; submit with the block shown and its reason, match tab with tolerances, variances and the override, payables tab; post, reverse, delete), EN/AR, with the Playwright journey extended. Tests (`Quicker.Purchasing.Tests`, +1, scenario 5): a USD order received and 4 of 10 sold, an invoice at 2.00 against 1.80 blocked on price (details at 11.11 %), a definition activated afterwards routing the same block to finance, the override granted with a comment and consumed on re-submission, posting that moves COGS by 4 × 260 and Inventory by 6 × 260, GRNI to zero, WHT 5 % and AP 76 USD in two instalments of 38, commitments consumed, the directory empty; a duplicate reference blocked; an expense invoice posted to its account; reversal restoring GRNI, COGS, commitments and closing the payables; the harness green throughout. |
| 4.5 Landed costs | done | Module `Quicker.Purchasing`, migration `V0027__purchasing_landed_costs.sql` (`pur_charge_types`, `pur_landed_cost_docs`, `pur_landed_cost_charges`, `pur_landed_cost_allocations`; invoice lines gain kind `charge`). **Charge types** per tenant (code, bilingual name, default allocation basis value/weight/volume/quantity, active flag; FREIGHT, CUSTOMS, DUTY, INSURANCE and HANDLING seeded as system rows that keep their code) with an API and an admin tab on the landed-costs screen. A **landed-cost document** (`LC-{yyyy}-{seq}`) names the posted receipt lines it lands on and its charges (type, amount in the document currency, the supplier expected to invoice it, a basis overriding the type's); each charge is allocated across the receipt lines by the basis (value = the receipt line's booked cost, quantity = base quantity, weight and volume = the item's per-unit figures × base quantity; a line without the figure refuses the basis) with `RoundingPolicy.Allocate`, so the parts sum to the charge in the company's currency to the minor unit (hard scenario 2 in IQD: 250 over 5 kg and 40 kg gives 27.778 and 222.222). `GET .../landed-costs/allocatable` lists the receipt lines with their value, quantity, weight and volume. **Posting** pushes every allocation onto the receipt line's stock entries (all of them for serialised lines) through the costing engine's `landed_cost` adjustment: Dr Inventory for what is still on hand, the cost adjustment run carrying the share of what was already consumed to cost of sales (PPV for standard-cost items), Cr `LandedCostClearing` with the document as its subledger item; each allocation and the document record the on-hand and sold portions (the **allocation report**). A landed cost is posted on or after the receipts it lands on. **Charge invoices**: a supplier invoice line of kind `charge` settles a charge of a posted document (the supplier must be the expected one when one was named; a charge is settled once): Dr `LandedCostClearing` (subledger item the document) / Cr AP; a difference between the estimate and the invoiced amount is allocated onto the same receipt lines in the same proportions (both directions), so the clearing account keeps only what is still estimated; reversing the invoice puts the estimate back. **Reversal** of a document whose charges are all still estimates takes every allocation back through the engine on or after its posting date; a settled document is refused. Permissions `purchasing.landed_cost.read|manage|post` (accountant and purchaser templates). `ItemInfo` gains the item's weight and volume. **Web**: `/purchasing/landed-costs` (documents with charges, basis, expected supplier and the receipt lines to land on; the allocation report with basis, allocated, to stock and to cost of sales per line; post, reverse, delete; a charge-types tab), and the invoice screen offers open charges as invoicable lines; EN/AR; the Playwright journey extended. Tests (`Quicker.Purchasing.Tests`, +1, scenario 2): 10 tea and 20 rice received on the 5th, half of each sold on the 10th, freight by value, handling by quantity and customs by weight posted on the 26th allocating 640 to the fils, 320 to stock and 320 to cost of sales (COGS and Inventory both +320, the clearing account −640); the forwarder's invoice settling freight at 320 against 300 (the 20 split 10/10 and half to cost of sales, the clearing account at −340, AP −320); reversal refused on the settled document; a second document posted and reversed to zero; drafts edited and deleted; the harness green throughout. |
| 4.6 Returns and debit notes | done | Module `Quicker.Purchasing`, migration `V0028__purchasing_returns.sql` (`pur_returns`, `pur_return_lines`, `ap_settlements`; invoice kind `debit_note`, invoice line kind `return`). A **supplier return** (`RTN-{yyyy}-{seq}`) is drafted against one posted receipt: lines name the receipt line, a quantity in any of the item's units (never more than received less already returned, `return.over_return`), the bin, lot and serials (the engine enforces the item's tracking), a line reason; the header carries the posting date (on or after the receipt's), the supplier's RMA, a reason, notes and custom fields (host `purchase_return`). `GET .../returns/returnable` lists posted receipt lines with what can still go back. **Posting** goes through the stock engine as a `purchase_return` applied to the receipt line's own entries, so the goods leave at exactly the cost they carry now (invoice price and landed costs included): Cr Inventory / Dr GRNI with the return as the GRNI subledger item; each line keeps its cost, and the receipt line its returned quantity and cost. **Reversal** (posted, uncredited returns only, with a reason, on or after the posting date) brings the goods back at the same cost against the return's entries and the receipt forgets the return. A **debit note** is a supplier invoice of kind `debit_note`: lines of kind `return` credit posted return lines (never more than went back, `invoice.return_over_credited`; only on a debit note, `invoice.return_needs_debit_note`; the receipt price offered as the default), expense lines credit an account; no withholding; it skips matching (nothing to match) and follows the invoice workflow. Posting books Dr AP (the note as subledger item, a negative open item of kind `debit_note`) / Cr GRNI at the returned goods' cost under the return's reference / the difference between what the supplier credits and that cost to `PurchasePriceVariance` (the goods are gone, nothing is left to re-price); the return lines record the credited quantity and amount; reversal mirrors it and is refused once the note was applied. **Credit application** (`POST .../invoices/open-items/apply`): a posted debit note settles one open invoice item of the same supplier and currency by an amount up to both remainders, recorded in `ap_settlements` (kind `credit_application`); where the two items were booked at different rates, the difference in the company's currency is realised FX booked Dr/Cr AP per item against FX gain or loss, so the payables control account still equals the open items; `GET .../invoices/settlements` lists them. **Invariant** `grni_matches_receipts` now counts GRNI lines referenced by a receipt or by any return of it and expects Σ (expected − invoiced − returned) + Σ credited of the receipt's posted returns. Permissions `purchasing.return.read|manage|post` (purchaser and warehouse-operator templates). API `/api/v1/purchasing/returns`. **Web**: `/purchasing/returns` (posted receipts with their lines, quantities, lot/serials, RMA, reasons, post, reverse, delete drafts; each line's cost and what was credited), the invoice screen gains the debit-note kind, returned lines as invoicable lines, an "apply credit" panel on a posted note's payables tab and the settlements of an item; EN/AR; the Playwright journey extended (two of the eight tea back at their landed cost, the note posted and applied to the invoice). Tests (`Quicker.Purchasing.Tests`, +1): ten tea received and invoiced, four returned at 4 000 (stock 6, GRNI 4 000 under the return, over-return and early dates refused), a note for three at the order price (GRNI 1 000 left, AP −7 000, the return credited 3/3 000, its reversal refused), the note applied to the invoice (7 000 left, the note settled, over-application, wrong kinds and closed items refused, both documents' reversal refused as settled), the last unit credited at 900 (GRNI 0, price variance 100), a second return reversed (stock and GRNI back, dates validated), the harness green at every step. |
| 4.7 Payables | done | Two new modules and one engine change. **Posting engine** (ADR-0031): a posting line may fix its functional-currency value (`AmountFc`), and a line with a functional amount and no transaction amount is a realised exchange difference; the schema check on `gl_journal_lines` allows it (migration `V0029`); `IPostingRules.EnsureRuleAsync` lets a module register the rule that routes a role to a keyed account. **Payables module** (`Quicker.Payables`, `.Contracts`, `.TestSupport`, `.Tests`; `ap_open_items` and `ap_settlements` moved here from Purchasing, which now opens and reverses its items through `IPayables`): open items per invoice instalment, debit note, advance and payment on account (signed, with booked rate and functional remainder); **settlements** as rows that move both items, with the settlement rate, discount, withholding, charge, write-off and realised FX, reversals as mirror rows naming what they reverse; **aging at any date** (`GET /payables/open-items/aging?asOf`) computed from the items posted by then and the settlements dated up to then, bucketed by days past due per supplier with advances apart, never from a snapshot (hard scenario 15); **holds** with a reason (proposals skip held items, nothing is applied to them); **applications** of a debit note, a payment on account or an advance to an invoice of the same supplier and currency, the rate difference booked as realised FX against each item's control (an advance moves from the supplier-advances control, now a control account with the payables subledger, to trade payables), reversible; **payment proposals** (`PP-{yyyy}-{seq}`): unheld invoice items due by the pay-through date or whose early-payment discount is still open, the discount worked out, lines deselected or reduced (a partial amount forfeits the discount), approved, cancelled, deleted, executed by the banking module. Permissions `payables.open_item.read|manage`, `payables.settlement.post`, `payables.proposal.read|manage|approve` (accountant template). **Banking module** (`Quicker.Banking`, `.Contracts`, `.TestSupport`, `.Tests`, migration `V0030`): **bank, cash and petty-cash accounts** on the chart's control account of their kind (or a named one) with the posting rule keyed on the account registered at creation, the **bank transactions** as their subledger and balance; **supplier payments** (`PAY-{yyyy}-{seq}`): lines settle open invoice items (amount up to the remainder, early-payment discount by the item's terms when paid in full within the window, tax withheld at payment by the supplier's code), a remainder on account, bank charges, the bank amount when the account is in the company's currency and the payment in another; posting relieves each item at its booked functional value, credits discount taken and withholding payable, debits charges, credits the bank at what it took, and books the functional difference as realised FX gain or loss (one journal, ADR-0031), opens the payment's own item (kind `payment_on_account`, the lines use it up, the rest stays open) and records one settlement per line; **advances** (kind `supplier_advance`) open an item on the advances control; **reversal** mirrors the journal, reopens the items at their booked values and mirrors the bank transaction, refused while the remainder was applied further; **pay a proposal** drafts one payment per supplier and marks the proposal executed. Permissions `banking.bank_account.read|manage`, `banking.payment.read|manage|post` (accountant template). **Invariants** `payables_match_open_items` (trade payables and advances controls equal their open items' functional remainders per company) and `bank_matches_transactions` (each bank account's control lines equal its transactions). **Web**: `/payables/open-items` (items by supplier, status and kind; aging at any date with buckets and totals; hold and release; apply and unapply), `/payables/proposals` (propose, edit lines, approve, cancel, pay), `/banking/bank-accounts` (accounts, balances, movements), `/banking/payments` (lines picked from the supplier's open invoices, on account, charges, bank amount, post, reverse); the invoice screen's payables tab now reads the payables module; EN/AR; a new Playwright journey (an invoice paid in part with a remainder on account, the remainder applied, aging, a proposal paid). Tests: `Quicker.Payables.Tests` (+1: aging at two dates equal to the control account, holds, a proposal created, edited, approved, cancelled, deleted) and `Quicker.Banking.Tests` (+1, hard scenario 3 on the payables side: EUR 1 000 booked at 1.10 paid 400 from a USD account at 1.08 with a 5 USD fee (8 gain) and 600 from a EUR account at 1.12 with a 2 EUR fee (12 loss), the second reversed; a local invoice on 2/10 net 30 with 5 % withheld at payment paid in cash within the window with 700 on account, applied to a second invoice, unapplied, the payment reversed; an advance applied; a proposal for both invoices with 180 discount paid by one payment of 8 370; the harness green at every step). |
| 4.8 Supplier intelligence | done | Module `Quicker.Purchasing`, migration `V0031__purchasing_intelligence.sql` (`pur_scoring_settings`). Read-only views computed from the documents as they stand (nothing snapshotted, so reversals show at once): **price history** (`GET .../intelligence/price-history`) from approved-or-later order lines, posted invoice lines and quotes, each point with its unit price after discount, currency and company-currency value (quotes carry none unless already in the company's currency), plus a summary per item and supplier (points, last, minimum, maximum, average) over what was actually bought; **lead times** (`.../lead-times`) from order date to receipt posting date per supplier (average, median, minimum, maximum, receipt lines) beside the supplier's stated lead time and the share received by the expected date (line's, else the order's); **scorecard** (`.../scorecard?asOf`) over a look-back window: on time (receipt lines by the expected date plus a tolerance), quantity kept (100 − returned ÷ received), price within tolerance (invoice lines whose variance is within the tolerance the match used) and invoices matched first time, weighted into a score (dimensions without data are left out and the weights re-normalised) and a grade A ≥ 90, B ≥ 75, C ≥ 60, D; **scoring settings** per company (weights, on-time tolerance days, look-back months; defaults 30/25/20/25, 0, 12) read and saved through `.../scoring-settings`, audited. Permissions `purchasing.intelligence.read|manage` (purchaser template). **Web**: `/purchasing/intelligence` (scorecard with the settings form, lead times, price history with an item filter), EN/AR; the purchasing journey reads all three tabs. Tests (`Quicker.Purchasing.Tests`, +1): two suppliers, one on time and one five days late with 2 of 10 returned; four price points and the per-supplier summary; lead times 3 and 7 days; scores 100 (A) and 65 (C) by default, 95 (A) with five days of tolerance; invalid settings refused; a scorecard before any receipt is empty; the harness green. |
| 4.9 Demo seed v4 | done | `DemoPurchasing` extends the same `demo` tenant after the stock, through the purchasing, partners, banking and payables services inside the seeder's unit of work (ASSUMPTIONS A-115). Per company (IQT beverages into Basra, USI accessories into the distribution centre, AEG stationery into the Dubai showroom): supplier accounts for three suppliers with stated lead times; eleven months of purchase orders, approved and then closed, with prices rising about one percent a month and each supplier pricing differently (99 orders, the year of price history behind supplier intelligence); then in the open current month an on-time supplier (received a day before the promised date, invoiced, freight landed two weeks after the receipt, paid in full), a late one (received five days after the promised date, ten units returned against a debit note, paid in half) and an open one (sixty of eighty received, invoiced, unpaid); an operating bank account the payments go out of; and an RFQ with three quotes left for comparison. Result counts and the audit entry carry orders, supplier invoices and payments; the demo test asserts eleven months of order history, the receipts and return in the stock ledger, the posted landed costs, the items left to age, the nine quotes, a green harness and determinism on reseed. |

### M5 slices

| Slice | Status | Notes |
|-------|--------|-------|
| 5.1 Customer master and CRM | done | Module `Quicker.Partners`, migration `V0039__customers_crm.sql` (8 tenant tables; the default pipeline back-filled for existing workspaces under each tenant's row-level security). **Customer accounts** per partner and company (ASSUMPTIONS A-142): group (posting group, payment and delivery terms the account defaults from), sales rep, default warehouse, currency, statements, and credit (limit in the functional currency or none, exposure basis, overdue days that block, status ok, on hold or blocked with reason, time and actor); the customer permission keeps terms, `partners.credit.manage` keeps credit, one endpoint for both; `ICustomerDirectory.EnsureCustomerAsync` refuses a blocked customer for quotes, orders, shipments and invoices and always takes a receipt. **Each side its own role**: the supplier role only with the supplier permission, the customer role only with the customer permission, a supplier's bank accounts only with the supplier permission (the creating-suppliers/paying-them segregation rests on it); each side sees its own accounts; reads stay within the member's companies. **Sales reps** (a workspace member, an employee partner, one company or all) and **commission plans** (paid on revenue, margin or collections, accruing at invoice or payment, marginal bands by item category (with descendants) and customer group from a period-to-date threshold; `quote` shows the matched scope, the bands crossed and the rounded commission; a property test proves splitting a sale or crediting it back never changes what it earns). **Pipeline** stages (open, won at 100 %, lost at 0 %; six system defaults; reorderable; always an active open, won and lost stage) and **opportunities** (`OPP-{yyyy}-{seq}` per company, a customer or a prospect, rep and currency from the account, custom fields under host `opportunity`, moves recorded with a per-deal sequence, lost with a reason, reopened by moving back; comments and files follow the company). **CRM activities** (call, meeting, email, task, note; assigned with an in-app notice; done with an outcome or cancelled). **Customer 360** (`GET /partners/{id}/customer-360`): the partner, its accounts, contacts, addresses, pipeline with win rate, open and recent activities, and each module's balances and documents through `IPartnerActivitySource` (payables now). **Web**: a "Sales and CRM" section (keys `s c`, `s p`, `s a`, `s t`): customers with filters (credit, rep, mine) and a new-customer dialog; the Customer 360 page (key figures, accounts with credit control, contacts and addresses, opportunities, activities, transactions, discussion, history); the pipeline board (drag a deal or use its menu; lost asks why; weighted totals per currency; days in stage; late closes flagged); the opportunity dialog (stage steps, win and lose, details, activities, stage history, discussion); the activities list (mine or everyone's, overdue first); sales set-up (customer groups, reps, commission plans with a "try it" calculator, stages with ordering); customers in the command palette; every string in English and Arabic. **Demo** (`DemoCrm`): the books' six customers become customers with contacts, addresses, VAT numbers for the Emirates ones and accounts in each company they buy from (Al-Noor on credit hold), four reps under a plan per company (Noor Abbas, the demo sales user, among them), six prospects, 23 deals in every stage across three companies and currencies, dated over four months, with the meetings, next steps (some late) and notes around them. Tests: 7 Partners tests (accounts and credit, role separation and company scope, commission quotes, pipeline and board, activities, the 360's composition and scope), 2 property tests of the commission arithmetic, a payables test of the 360's payables panel, the demo seeder's CRM checks, and the acceptance check: **Customer 360 answers in under 300 ms at the 95th percentile on the demo tenant** (120 requests through the API, `Customer360Tests`); browser journey `sales-journeys.spec.ts` in English and Arabic with axe. |
| 5.2 Pricing engine | done | New core module `Quicker.Pricing` (tier 1; ARCHITECTURE §6), migration `V0040__pricing.sql` (10 tenant tables: price lists with assignments to customers and groups, list prices, customer agreements, discount rules, promotions with components, tiers and usages, floors). **Engine** (`PriceEngine`, pure, A-144, ADR-0030 amended): base price from a typed price, the customer's agreement, the document's list, the customer's lists, the group's lists, the default list or the item's list price; quantity breaks per unit (base-unit prices converted exactly), variants, validity; derived lists across currencies with a percentage, a rounding increment (nearest, up, down) and a surcharge; exclusive discounts compete and stackable ones follow by priority; buy X get Y (the goods given as their own line), bundles, volume tiers and coupons over the basket; document discounts allocated by largest remainder; floors on price and margin; every line explained step by step with the candidates considered. **API** `/api/v1/pricing`: price lists (+ prices, yearly adjustment), agreements, discount rules, promotions, floors, and `POST /calculate`; promotion usage recorded under a row lock for 5.4. Permissions `pricing.price.read`, `pricing.price_list|agreement|promotion|floor.manage`, `pricing.price.override`; sales-manager and sales-rep templates. Kernel: `RoundingPolicy.RoundToMultiple` and `WholeTimes`. Tests (`Quicker.Pricing.Tests`, 16): each step of the pipeline, 1,500 generated rule sets and baskets priced twice and with every rule collection reordered (identical results; every line and total adds up), hard scenario 14 through the API (a customer's quantity-break price in dinars on a dollar document with a volume promotion, identical on a second call), management and validation, company scope and the override permission, one promotion use taken by one of two documents confirmed at once. **Web** (A-145): Sales › Price lists (list, a page per list with its prices, breaks, validity, who it is for, its derivation and a yearly increase), Discounts and promotions (customer agreements, discount rules with scope, conditions and weekdays, promotions of the four kinds, floors), Price check (a basket for any customer, date, currency, list, channel and coupons, with the "why this price" panel per line and the basket's promotions and document discounts); shortcuts `s l`, `s r`, `s k`; EN/AR. **Demo**: a standard list per company, four derived lists, Basra Oil's carton contract, category discounts, four rules, four promotions, three floors (`DemoPricing`, checked by the demo seeder test). Browser journey `e2e/pricing-journeys.spec.ts` (a standard list with breaks, a derived wholesale list for a customer, buy-ten-get-a-glass, a floor, the price check's explanation, a typed discount breaking the floor, then Arabic; axe on every screen). |
| 5.3 Tax engine | done | **5.3a, the engine** (A-146, ADR-0018 amended): new core module `Quicker.Tax` (tier 1), migration `V0041__tax.sql` (9 tenant tables; items, categories and customer and supplier accounts now reference tax groups). **Country templates** as versioned data files: Saudi Arabia (5% then 15% from 1 July 2020, ZATCA return boxes, reverse charge on imported services, imports at customs, exemption reason codes), UAE (VAT 201 boxes, reverse charge on imported services and goods), Bahrain (5% then 10% from 2022), Oman, Qatar and Kuwait (no VAT), Jordan (16% GST), Egypt (14%), Turkey (18% then 20% from July 2023), Iraq's selective sales tax (hospitality 10%, telecoms 20%, not recoverable), a generic EU set; installing one creates the regime, codes with dated rates, boxes and account roles, and its matrix, reusing the tenant's tax groups by code. **Determination** (`ITaxDetermination`): the company's registration in force, a customer's exemption certificate, then the most specific matrix row (item group, partner group, ship-from, ship-to, then the latest start); refused with the facts when no row matches; a line may name its code. **Calculator**: prices excluding or including tax, per-line or per-document rounding (the regime's or the company's choice), reverse charge on the net and outside the gross. **Tax ledger** (`ITaxLedger`, `tax_entries` append-only): once per document, refused in a filed return period, reversed by opposite rows, serialised against filing by a lock on the registration. **API** `/api/v1/tax`: templates (list, install), regimes with codes, rates and matrix, groups, registrations, exemptions, and `POST /calculate` with the reason for every line's code. Permissions `tax.setup.read|manage`, `tax.return.read|file`; the accountant template has `tax.*`, the sales, purchasing and clerk templates read the set-up. Items and partners refuse a tax group of the wrong kind (`ITaxGroupDirectory`); an item's tax group falls back to its category's. Tests (`Quicker.Tax.Tests`, 14): the calculator's worked examples and 3,000 generated documents (every line adds up on either basis and level, a code's tax is its lines' tax rounded once, the order of lines changes nothing, a credit note mirrors its invoice); Saudi VAT through the API (goods, services, zero-rated by item and by category, exports, the rate of the date, tax-inclusive prices, an unclassified item refused); imported services and goods self-assessed, an exemption certificate and its dates, a chosen code, one set of groups for two countries; UAE per-line and per-document rounding by company and by regime, imports reverse charged into boxes 3, 6 and 10; set-up validation; a scoped member; the ledger's once-only, filed-period, reversal and append-only rules. **5.3b, purchases** (A-147, migration `V0042__purchasing_tax.sql`): purchase orders and supplier invoices taxed by the engine on their dates, each line keeping its code, rate, reverse charge, recoverability and reason and the document its rounding level; a code chosen on the order carries to the invoice; an unclassified item is refused once the company is registered, itemless expense and charge lines take the templates' new default purchase rows (standard, reverse charged from abroad); posting puts recoverable tax on input tax by code (with its base), self-assessed tax on input and output tax, and non-recoverable tax into the line's cost (the stock value for goods, GRNI still clearing); every taxed line writes the tax ledger, tied to the journal's tax lines to the halala; a posting in a filed period is refused and writes nothing; reversals take the tax back. Iraq's hotel and telecom sales tax is recorded on purchases as non-recoverable. Tests: `PurchaseTaxTests` (order and invoice VAT, reverse charge in dollars, entertainment VAT not recoverable, goods on a non-recoverable code valued with the tax, the ledger tying to input and output tax, reversal, a filed September refusing a posting, the default rows and the unclassified item), the purchasing, payables and banking suites unchanged. **5.3b, pricing** (A-148): a price found on the other tax basis than the document's is converted at the item's tax rate for the customer on the pricing date, shown as its own "tax basis converted" step (both bases, rate, code) in the price explanation in English and Arabic; only an item the tax engine cannot determine is still refused. **5.3c, the return, filing and e-invoicing** (A-149, ADR-0018 amended again, migration `V0043__tax_returns.sql`): the return for a company, regime and period is computed fresh from the tax ledger, box by box, reconciled to the tax accounts of the general ledger through a new Accounting read contract `ILedgerReader.TaxMovementAsync` (net movement by tax code and account role, so Tax never reads Accounting's own tables); drill-down lists the entries behind one box; filing takes the exclusive lock a posting only shares on the company's registration, so PostgreSQL serialises the two with no torn state, refuses an unreconciled return, refuses overlapping an already-filed range, and books the figures filed into the period's totals. `UblExportService` builds the generic UBL 2.1 export every tenant gets from a document's tax ledger entries (header, the two parties, the tax summary by code); `ITaxClearanceRegistry` wires `ITaxClearanceProvider` end to end, resolved by the regime's e-invoicing scheme — no ZATCA, Peppol or ETA adapter ships yet, so a regime naming a scheme is refused honestly (`tax.clearance_not_configured`) rather than a placeholder success, and one naming none needs no clearance at all. API: `GET /returns` (list), `GET /returns/preview`, `GET /returns/drilldown`, `POST /returns/file`, `GET /documents/{type}/{id}/ubl`, `POST /documents/{type}/{id}/clearance/submit`. Tests (`TaxReturnTests`, 5): boxes and reconciliation from two invoices, drill-down, filing and its lock against re-filing and a late posting; the exclusive lock genuinely racing a posting (either order leaves the filed totals and the posting's own outcome consistent, never torn); filing refused when a row posted outside the tax module breaks reconciliation; the UBL export's header, parties and tax summary, and clearance wired but honestly unconfigured for Saudi Arabia (ZATCA) and not required for a schemeless regime; permission scoping (`tax.return.read` previews and drills down, only `tax.return.file` files). Accounting, purchasing, architecture, schema and invariant suites unchanged. **5.3d, the screens, journey and demo**: `routes/tax/` — Tax setup (Templates with an install button and code/rule counts; Regimes with an inline regime detail panel — settings, its codes table with rates and an add/edit dialog carrying account roles, the determination matrix with an add/edit/delete dialog; Groups with a kind filter; Registrations and Exemptions scoped by company) and Tax returns (company and regime pickers, a period range defaulting to the current month, the preview's boxes and reconciliation-to-ledger tables with a reconciled/not-reconciled badge, per-box drill-down in a dialog, a reference field and file button disabled once reconciled fails or the period is already filed, and the company's filed periods). Nav: "Tax setup" (`Percent`, `g {`) and "Tax returns" (`Stamp`, `g }`) join the "finance" group, renamed "Payables, banking and tax". Full `tax` i18n section, English and Arabic. Browser journey `e2e/tax-journeys.spec.ts`: install Iraq's sales-tax template, extend its codes and matrix (a new code and rule alongside the template's own), add a partner tax group, register the company, then preview and file an empty period (trivially reconciled with nothing posted) and see it locked; then the same in Arabic; axe on every screen. **Demo** (`DemoTax`, A-150): Iraq sales tax installed once for IQT and USI, UAE VAT installed once for AEG, each company registered with its real-looking tax number from `DemoData` and a two-year-back registration date; this runs after `DemoPurchasing`, so the live month's existing purchase history predates the regime exactly as a business going live mid-year would carry unposted history without tax codes — the return for that period is legitimately empty and reconciled, not a stub. Demo seeder test asserts two regimes and three registrations, deterministic on reseed. |
| 5.6 Invoicing | done | **5.6a, receivables subledger, invoices and credit notes** (A-159): new process module `Quicker.Receivables` (tier 2) with contracts, mirroring Payables with the signs the other way round: `ar_open_items`/`ar_settlements` (migration `V0053__receivables_and_sales_invoices.sql`; a positive item is owed by the customer), `IReceivables` (open, find, items of a document, reverse a document, record a settlement a journal carries, apply a credit or deposit with realised FX booked by the subledger itself, reverse a receipt's settlements, a customer's open balance in a currency), aging at any date computed from items and settlements, customer statements with opening balance and reversals, holds, applications and their reversal (`/api/v1/receivables/open-items`, `/aging`, `/statement`, `/settlements`), permissions `receivables.open_item.read|manage`, `receivables.settlement.post` and the forward-declared `receivables.writeoff.post` (so the default SoD rule pairing it with `sales.invoice.post` does not go dead now that `receivables.*` has shipped; 5.7 wires it). `ReceivablesJournalSubledger` follows manual, opening and correcting journals on the receivables and customer-deposits controls (A-140 on the customer side: a line names a customer, `journal.customer_required` otherwise; a deposit on the customer-deposits account, which the chart templates now mark as a receivables control like supplier advances); the partner 360 view gains the receivable side. New invariant `receivables_match_open_items` (receivables and deposits controls equal their open items per company) runs in the harness after every scenario. Sales invoices and credit notes in `Quicker.Sales` (`sls_invoices`/`sls_invoice_lines`, `InvoiceService`, `/api/v1/sales/invoices` with `invoicable`, create, update, cancel, post, reverse; permissions `sales.invoice.read|manage|post`, numbered `INV-{yyyy}-{seq:5}` and `CN-{yyyy}-{seq:5}` at creation, a never-posted draft cancelled with its number kept): lines bill posted shipment lines (in part, and consolidated across shipments and orders of one customer and currency) and service lines of orders at the *order's* price — each invoice takes its share of the line's net and the last one the exact remainder (`net_invoiced` on order lines, `qty_invoiced` on shipment lines); non-stock items are priced here by the pricing engine (a stock item on a direct invoice is refused, `invoice.stock_item_needs_shipment`, until direct stock invoices move the stock with the invoice); free service lines name an account role (default `Revenue`), their price, what they are for and, having no item to take a tax group from, their tax code; a credit note credits a posted invoice's lines (at that line's price and tax, `qty_credited`/`net_credited`) or stands on its own, and one naming the invoice it credits is applied to that invoice's open items as soon as it posts (oldest due first) through the subledger's own application, so a later rate difference is realised FX. Tax runs through the tax engine on the document date with the order's or the credited line's code pinned. Posting: Dr `AR` gross / Cr `Revenue` (or the line's role) per line with the line's dimensions / Cr output tax per code (reverse charge reported in the tax ledger, never booked), the tax ledger, then one open item per payment-terms instalment with the functional value allocated from the receivables line actually booked (so the control equals its items to the minor unit, where Purchasing rounds each instalment on its own); reversal mirrors the journal and tax ledger, closes the items (refused while one is settled, `invoice.settled`) and gives the billed and credited quantities back. Credit exposure (A-152) now reads real receivables: `open_ar` = the customer's open items net of unapplied credits plus this order; `open_ar_plus_orders` adds the uninvoiced part of its other confirmed orders. Demo: the books' customers become partner records before the first journal names them (the CRM seed completes them), as the suppliers already did. Tests: `Quicker.Receivables.Tests` (+3: journal-opened items with a supplier refused, credit and deposit applications and their reversal, a settled item guarded; a USD credit applied at another rate booking 800 IQD realised gain with aging equal to the control at three dates; hold, release, statement and the 360 panel) and `Quicker.Sales.Tests` (+3: a partial, consolidated invoice from two shipments posting receivables, revenue and tax, the remainder invoice, reversal giving the quantity back; a credit note applied as it posts and both documents guarded until unapplied; a direct USD invoice with two instalments whose functional values sum to the booked line, counting against the credit limit, read-only member, cancelled draft) — every scenario ends with the invariant harness. Accounting tests that posted AR lines against made-up ids now name real customers. Deferred to 5.6b/5.6c, listed explicitly: recurring invoices, deferred revenue schedules, commission accrual at invoice, the e-invoice status (column `einvoice_status` is `not_required` until the regime's clearance scheme is wired), direct invoices of stock items, invoice and receivables screens, journey and demo invoices. **5.6b, recurring invoices, deferred revenue, commissions and the e-invoice status** (A-160, migration `V0054__sales_invoicing_extensions.sql`): (1) a line with `deferralPeriods` (1–120, from `deferralStartsOn` or the document date) parks its net on `DeferredRevenue` at posting instead of revenue and asks Accounting for a `deferred_revenue` schedule through a new contract `IDeferralSchedules` (the engine of 2.7, nothing new there: the balance account is the one the journal actually parked the net on, the target the chart's account for the line's revenue role; the daily routines release each month, Dr deferred revenue / Cr revenue); reversing the invoice cancels what is still planned and is refused once a period was recognised (`invoice.deferral_recognised`: credit the invoice instead); a credit note is never deferred. (2) Commission accrual at invoice (POSTING_RULES §2): when the invoice's sales rep (the customer account's by default) has an active plan accruing at invoice on revenue or margin, each line's basis in the plan's currency (revenue, or a shipped line's net less its cost of goods sold; converted from the company's currency at the posting-date spot rate when the plan's currency differs) is quoted through `ICustomerDirectory.QuoteCommissionAsync` against what the rep earned so far in the plan's tier period (calendar month, quarter or year of the posting date, from `sls_commission_entries`), and the total is booked in one journal Dr `CommissionExpense` / Cr `CommissionPayable` in the plan's currency (source document type `sales_commission`); a credit note's negative basis gives back what the same bands earned; reversal mirrors the journal and marks the entries reversed; plans accruing at payment or on collected amounts wait for 5.7's receipts. New contract `ICustomerDirectory.FindCommissionPlanAsync`. (3) Recurring invoices (`sls_recurring_invoices`, `/api/v1/sales/recurring-invoices`: list, create, get, update, generate, run): a customer's item and service lines on a cron schedule in the company's time zone (the `CronExpression` of the platform schedules), `startsOn`/`endsOn`, `autoPost` or a draft to review, `customerReference` and payment terms carried to each invoice, which records the template that generated it; `generate` issues the next due run (or a given date), `run` and the platform schedule `sales.invoices.recur` (seed `S0011`, 00:40 UTC, job `RecurringInvoiceJob` over every active tenant) generate every run due, one invoice per due date catching up at most a year, a failed run waiting with its reason; the schedule ends when the cron passes `endsOn` (`recurring_invoice.ended`). (4) E-invoice status: a draft keeps its regime's scheme from the tax engine (`TaxedDocumentResult.EinvoicingScheme`, new); posting sets `einvoice_status` to `pending` under a scheme and `not_required` without one; `POST /sales/invoices/{id}/einvoice/submit` sends the posted invoice to the tax module's clearance through a new contract `ITaxClearance` (the UBL export and the registered adapter) and records the scheme's answer (status, submission id, QR payload, messages, submitted at); with no adapter registered the submission is refused (`tax.clearance_not_configured`) and the status stays `pending`, as the ZATCA/Peppol/ETA adapters are still to come. Tests (`Quicker.Sales.Tests`, +4 in `InvoicingExtensionsTests`): a year of support deferred over 12 periods with onboarding billed at once (Dr AR 1,725 / Cr deferred revenue 1,200 / Cr revenue 300 / Cr output tax 225, a 12-line schedule from 2180 to 4100), a second deferred invoice reversed before recognition cancelling its schedule, the routines releasing September's 100 and the invoice then refused reversal; a rep's plan at 2% to 1,000 a month and 3% above earning 16 on 800 and 16 again on 600, a 600 credit note giving back 16 on its way down the bands, a reversal reversing its accrual and October starting the tiers afresh; a monthly recurring invoice issued by hand for September, the run catching up October and November and stopping at the end date; and a posted invoice under SA-VAT (scheme zatca) pending with submission refused until an adapter exists — all under the invariant harness. **5.6c, screens, journey and demo**: *Sales invoices* (`/sales/invoices`, nav `s i`): list by status and kind; a new invoice or credit note picks the customer's not-yet-invoiced shipment and order lines (added one by one, quantity editable for a partial invoice) or, for a credit note, the posted invoice lines left to credit, and takes item and service lines typed here with their price, discount, tax code (the company's primary regime's sales codes, or the item's own determination), months to defer and cost centre; the order and shipment screens' own "create invoice" button opens it pre-filled from the source document (`?from=sales_shipment:id` / `sales_order:id`); the detail shows lines with their deferral, the open items and settlements of the receivable, the commission entries, the e-invoice panel (scheme, status, submission, QR messages, submit) and the discussion and history tabs, with post, reverse (reason), cancel draft and edit. *Recurring invoices* (`/sales/recurring-invoices`): templates with cron presets (monthly, quarterly, yearly, weekly, custom), start and end, auto-post, item and service lines, the next run and what was issued, "issue now". *Receivables* (`/receivables/open-items`, finance group): open items filtered by customer and status, each with its settlements, hold and release, a credit or deposit applied to an invoice from the item and unapplied again; aging at any date per customer with deposits beside; a customer's statement for a period per currency with opening, invoiced, credited and received, closing, and every line linked to its document. Journey `invoicing-journeys.spec.ts` (a shipment invoiced from its own screen with a delivery charge, posted, two units credited and applied, the receivables screen's items, aging and statement, a recurring retainer issued; then Arabic with axe). Demo (`DemoInvoicing`): Baghdad Mall's posted shipment billed and posted with the rep's commission accrued, two units credited for the damaged carton and applied, Kurdistan Distribution's year of shelf placement deferred over twelve months beside a design fee, Al-Noor's delivery charge as a draft, Basra Oil's monthly retainer on a schedule with this month's invoice issued as a draft; the demo suite checks the counts, statuses, the applied credit, the schedule and the accrual, and the invariant harness. |
| 5.7 Receivables | done | **5.7a, customer receipts and deposits, write-offs, promises to pay** (A-161, migration `V0055__receivables_receipts_and_write_offs.sql`). *Receipts* live in Banking beside the supplier payments (`CustomerReceiptService`, kinds `customer_receipt` and `customer_deposit` on `bnk_payments`, their own series `RCT-{yyyy}-{seq:5}`, `/api/v1/banking/receipts`: list, create, get, update, delete, post, reverse; permissions `banking.receipt.read|manage|post`, granted to the receivables clerk template with bank-account reading): a receipt's lines settle a customer's open invoice items in one currency (an amount up to the remainder, the early-payment discount given by the item's terms when collected in full within the window, the tax the customer withheld by the code named on the receipt), the rest stays on account, the bank's charges are what it kept and the bank amount what it credited (required when the account is in the company's currency and the receipt in another); posting relieves each item at its booked functional value, debits discount given and withholding receivable, debits charges, debits the bank at what it credited and books the functional difference as realised FX gain or loss (one journal, ADR-0031 on the customer side), opens the receipt's own item (kind `receipt_on_account`, the lines use it up, the rest stays open) and records one settlement per line; a deposit opens an item on the customer-deposits control, applied to invoices later through the subledger's own application; reversal mirrors the journal, reopens the items at their booked values and mirrors the bank transaction, refused while the remainder was applied further (`receipt.applied`). A receipt is always taken, from a held or blocked customer too. *Write-offs* (`ar_write_offs`, `WO-{yyyy}-{seq:5}`, `POST /receivables/open-items/{id}/write-off`, `/receivables/write-offs` list, get, reverse; permission `receivables.writeoff.post`, already paired with `sales.invoice.post` in the default SoD rule): what is open on an invoice item, or part of it, as `bad_debt` (Dr `BadDebt`) or `small_balance` (Dr `WriteOff`) against receivables at the item's booked value in the item's currency (nothing is realised: no cash moved), opening a credit item of the write-off's own that settles the invoice item at once (settlement kind `write_off`, so the control still equals its items); reversal mirrors the journal, reopens the item for what was written off and retires the credit item; a write-off's settlement is reversed only through the write-off (`settlement.reverse_document`). *Promise to pay* (`POST /receivables/open-items/{id}/promise`, `receivables.open_item.manage`): the date the customer promised and what they said, cleared with a null date, refused in the past; dunning (5.7b) leaves a promised item alone until the date passes. Tests: `Quicker.Banking.Tests` (+1, `ReceiptTests`: a EUR invoice in a USD company collected in two instalments at 1.08 and 1.12 with the bank keeping 5 USD and 2 EUR, 8 loss then 12 gain, the second reversed; a local invoice on 2/10 net 30 collected in cash within the window with 5 % withheld and 700 on account, the remainder applied, unapplied and the receipt reversed; a deposit applied from its control; aging equal to what is owed) and `Quicker.Receivables.Tests` (+1: a promise recorded and cleared, 300 written off as a small balance and reversed, the whole balance as bad debt, the statement showing both and the reversal; the clerk without the permission refused) — every step under the invariant harness. **5.7b, dunning and netting** (A-162, migration `V0056__receivables_dunning_and_netting.sql`). *Dunning* (`ar_dunning_levels`, `ar_dunning_runs`, `ar_dunning_letters`; `/api/v1/receivables/dunning/levels` list, create, update; `/preview`; `/runs` list, issue, get; `/letters` list, mark sent; permissions `receivables.dunning.read|manage`): a company's reminder ladder of levels 1–9 by days past due, in order (a higher level needs more days), each with its bilingual name and wording, a fee in the company's currency and whether it puts the customer on credit hold; a run on a date (today or earlier) issues one letter per customer with an overdue, unheld, unpromised invoice item that climbs a rung since its last letter (a promise to pay keeps an item out until its date passes; deposits and held items are never dunned), the letter naming every overdue item of theirs as it stood (remaining, days late, the level reached) at the level of the highest, numbered `DUN-{yyyy}-{seq:5}-nn`, charging the level's fee as a receivable adjustment item of the letter's own (Dr `AR` / Cr `InterestIncome`, due on the run date, dunned in turn like anything owed) and recording the level on the items (`dunning_level`, `last_dunned_on`) and on the customer's account, which goes on credit hold when the level says so (`ICustomerDirectory.RecordDunningAsync`); a run with nothing climbing is refused (`dunning.nothing_due`); the preview shows the same letters without writing; letters are the record the print pipeline (5.9) will render. *Netting* (`ar_nettings`, `NET-{yyyy}-{seq:5}`, `/api/v1/receivables/nettings` list, create, get, reverse; `receivables.settlement.post`; A-113 fulfilled): a partner who is both customer and supplier has an open receivable invoice set against an open payable invoice in their common currency for an amount up to both remainders (the smaller by default): one journal Dr `AP` / Cr `AR` relieving each item at its booked functional value with the difference as realised FX, a counter item on each subledger (an `adjustment` owed by the company on receivables, one owed by the supplier on payables, opened through `IPayables`) used up at once by a settlement of kind `netting` on each side, so both controls keep equalling their items; reversal mirrors the journal and reopens both invoices (payables' guard refuses while the counter item was used further). Tests (`Quicker.Receivables.Tests`, +2): a two-rung ladder with a fee and a hold, an out-of-order rung refused, a run on the 15th issuing one letter at level 2 with the fee item opened, the control up by the fee and the customer on hold while the other customer's promised item stays out, a run a week later naming the recent invoice and the fee itself, nothing due afterwards, a reader refused; a partner netted for 600 of 1 000 against 600 owed, both subledgers settled and the controls agreeing, then reversed — under the invariant harness. **5.7c, screens, journey and demo**: *Customer receipts* (`/banking/receipts`, finance group, shortcut `r c`): receipts and deposits listed by status; a new receipt picks the customer, the account the money came into, date, method, reference, currency and rate, what stays on account (or the deposit amount), the bank's charges, what the bank credited when its currency differs, and the code of the tax the customer withheld, then the customer's open invoice items added one by one with the amount collected on each; the detail shows the amounts (discount given, withheld, on account, charges, credited), the lines with cash per line, the discussion and history, with edit, delete draft, post and reverse. *Receivables* (`/receivables/open-items`, shortcut `r o`): the item detail now carries the dunning level and the promise, and gains *promise to pay* (date and note, or cleared), *net against a payable* (the same partner's open supplier invoices in the currency, the smaller remainder proposed) and *write off* (amount, bad debt or small balance, reason); a *Dunning* tab (for `receivables.dunning.read`) shows the reminder ladder with add and edit (level, days past due, bilingual name and letter text, fee, credit hold, active), the letters due on a chosen date as a preview with "issue letters", the runs issued and, per run, its letters with the items each names, the fee and the hold, and "mark as sent". Document links resolve `bank_receipt` to the receipts screen. Journey `receivables-journeys.spec.ts` (an invoice dated five months back collected in part by transfer with the bank's charge, the promise recorded and cleared, 40 written off as a small balance, a two-rung ladder set up and a run issued that charges its fee, the fee item and the aging read, then Arabic with axe). Demo (`DemoReceivables`): Baghdad Mall's invoice collected in two transfers (the bank keeping 2,500 on the first) with the odd dinars written off as a small balance, Kurdistan Distribution's 5,000,000 deposit on the deposits control, Iraq Trading's three-rung ladder (reminder, second notice with a 25,000 fee, final notice with a 50,000 fee) and one run reminding Baghdad Mall of the year-old sale from the books, charging the final notice's fee; the demo suite checks the counts, statuses and settlements, and the invariant harness.|
| 5.8 Returns | done | **5.8a, returns backend** (A-163, migration `V0057__sales_returns.sql`): `sls_returns`/`sls_return_lines`, `ReturnService` in `Quicker.Sales`, `/api/v1/sales/returns` (list, `returnable`, create, get, update, delete, `authorise`, `reject`, `receive`, `inspect`, `credit`, `close`, `reverse`), numbered `RMA-{yyyy}-{seq:5}`, permissions `sales.return.read|manage|receive|credit` (the warehouse operator template gets read and receive, the receivables clerk read and credit, the sales manager all). A return is requested against a customer's posted shipment lines (quantities in the base unit, serials named for a serialised item and checked against what the line shipped and what came back already, an inventory reason code of kind `return` with its note rule, a proposed disposition, a bin, the cost policy `original | current | specified`), authorised or rejected, then *received*: each part is applied to the shipment's own ledger entries (serials to their entry, quantities in the shipment's order skipping what earlier returns took) and posted as `sale_return` with `AppliesToSleId` under the original policy, so the goods come back at exactly the cost they left with (Dr `Inventory` / Cr `Cogs`); the lot's status at that moment is recorded (`lot_status`, `lotBlocked`), a recalled, quarantined or expired lot comes back on hold by the stock engine's rule and its disposition is forced to quarantine; a serial comes back as `returned`. *Inspection* settles the dispositions: restock puts a returned serial back in stock, repair puts it in repair (both through the new `ITrackingDirectory` contract), scrap posts `scrap` lines applied to the return's entries (Dr `Scrap` / Cr `Inventory` at the return's cost), quarantine keeps the goods where they were put; a blocked lot refuses restock and repair (`return.lot_blocked`). *Credit* issues one credit note per invoice that billed the returned lines at the invoice's own price, discount and tax on the `SalesReturns` role, posted and applied to the invoice at once (`InvoiceService` now honours an `accountRole` on a credited line and records the return on the note, which is reversed only through the return, `invoice.credited_by_return`); returned goods never invoiced need no credit and are not invoiced later (`sls_shipment_lines.qty_returned`/`qty_return_credited`, honoured by `invoicable` and the invoice's quantity check); nothing to credit is `return.nothing_to_credit`. *Reverse* undoes a received return as a whole (credit notes first, refused while one is settled; refused once anything was scrapped, `return.disposed`): `sale_shipment` lines applied to the return's entries take the goods out again. New contracts in Inventory: `ITrackingDirectory` (a lot, a serial by number, the repair chain of a serial) and `IReasonCodeDirectory` (a reason code by code). Tests (`Quicker.Sales.Tests`, +3 in `ReturnTests`): hard scenario 12 (a lot sold and invoiced, recalled, three units back at the shipment's 2 each on hold with the recall recorded, restock refused, quarantined, credited 15 on `SalesReturns` and applied to the invoice, the note refused reversal by hand and the return refused reversal while applied, closed); hard scenario 13 (a serial sold, one unit invoiced, SN-1 back as returned at 200, into repair with its stock on hold, back in stock through the serial, the uninvoiced return leaving nothing to invoice until the credit note credits the invoiced unit, resold as the only unit on hand, the history reading in_stock → sold → returned → in_repair → in_stock → sold); scrap at 160 with its journal and the return pinned, a rejected request, a two-unit return with nothing to credit reversed, a reader refused — every step under the invariant harness. **5.8b, screen, journey and demo**: *Sales returns* (`/sales/returns`, sales group, shortcut `s u`): returns listed by status with requested and received dates, cost value and what was credited; a new return picks the customer, the request date, the cost policy, the reason and the customer's reference, then adds the customer's returnable shipment lines one by one (what was shipped and returned so far, the quantity coming back, the serials for a serialised line, a return reason code of the inventory catalogue, free text, the proposed disposition and, under the specified policy, the unit cost); a posted shipment's own "Return goods" button opens it pre-filled with every remaining unit of that shipment. The detail shows the lines with the shipment link, lot and its status when received (a recalled lot flagged), serials, reason, disposition and note, cost and scrap value and what was credited, the credit notes linked to the invoices screen, the discussion and history tabs, and drives the whole flow by permission: edit, delete, authorise and reject (reason) for `sales.return.manage`, receive (date) and inspect (a disposition and note per line, confirmed together) and reverse (reason) for `sales.return.receive`, issue credit note for `sales.return.credit`, close. Clicking a returned serial opens its whole chain on the return itself (every movement and status change with its source document), so sold → returned → in repair → in stock → sold reads on one screen (hard scenario 13). Every string in English and Arabic; document links resolve `sales_return`. Journey `returns-journeys.spec.ts` (a recalled lot's three units requested, authorised, received on hold with the recall flagged and the disposition forced to quarantine, restock refused at inspection, quarantined, credited 15 on the invoice and closed; a phone back from the shipment's own button for repair with its chain on the return; then Arabic with axe). Demo (`DemoReturns`): Baghdad Mall's six units of groceries back near their date, quarantined and credited, three crushed cartons scrapped at the return's cost and credited (the invoice already collected, so the credits stay on account), one request waiting to be authorised, with the return reason codes the warehouse uses; the demo suite checks the counts, statuses, the scrap and the credit notes, and the invariant harness. |
| 5.9 Print pipeline | done | **5.9a, print backend** (ADR-0022, migration `V0058__print_pipeline.sql`): `prt_templates` and `prt_template_versions` (the shipped bilingual invoice, pro forma, order and statement templates copied in on first use; every edit a new version, rollback, copies by company or branch), `prt_renders` (headless Chromium in the worker, cached by fingerprint, a final document's first PDF kept as evidence under retention) and `prt_emails` (the PDF attached, the send logged on the document); a draft invoice refuses to print as a tax invoice. **5.9b, print and send** (A-176, migration `V0062__print_share_links.sql`): the print dialog on sales invoices (tax invoice, or pro forma for a draft), sales orders and the customer statement: the preview from the template, print and download of the PDF the worker makes, email with the PDF, WhatsApp and plain share links that open without signing in (`prt_share_links`: hashed tokens, expiry, revocation, opening counts, `GET /api/v1/public/prints/{token}`), and the history of PDFs, emails and links; `/print-templates` with the design (HTML and CSS as versions), settings, preview from sample data, versions with rollback and copies; permission `print.document.share` and print grants in the role templates; the fingerprint without the generation time; journey `print-journeys.spec.ts`. |
| 5.5 Pick, pack, ship | done | **5.5a, shipments core** (A-153): new tables `sls_shipments`/`sls_shipment_lines` (migration `V0047__sales_shipments.sql`) against a confirmed (or `partially_shipped`) order's lines. Posting (`ShipmentService.PostAsync`) greedily consumes one or more of a line's *active* reservations (oldest first, `IStockReservations.ForDocumentAsync`) and calls `IInventoryPosting.PostAsync` with `StockEntryTypes.SaleShipment` — the first real caller of the costing engine's already-built `AccountRoles.Cogs` mapping (roadmap 3.3) and of the stock engine's partial-reservation-consumption path (built for hard scenario 4, never exercised before this slice); nothing new on the value or GL side, only the document. A shipment line's quantity is always the item's base unit (extending A-152's `qty_reserved`/`qty_shipped` reasoning, no unit conversion needed); exceeding what a line has reserved is refused (`shipment.exceeds_reserved`, re-checked at draft-save and post time); a drop-ship line is refused (`shipment.line_is_drop_ship`, nothing to pick — its own fulfilment stays a later gap); a lot- or serial-tracked item is refused explicitly (`shipment.tracked_item_unsupported`) rather than half-handled. A line reaching zero remaining becomes `fulfilled`; the order becomes `shipped` once every non-cancelled, non-drop-ship line is fulfilled, `partially_shipped` once any has shipped. Reversal (`ReverseAsync`, mirroring `ReceiptService.ReverseAsync`) posts `StockEntryTypes.SaleReturn` against the original entries at their exact cost, then re-reserves the returned quantity for the order through the same full/partial fallback `OrderService.ReserveLineAsync` already uses.
