Purchasing: migration `V0072__trader_offer_lines.sql` (`moq`, `notes` on `pur_supplier_quote_lines`), quote lines carry a minimum order quantity and a note, the comparison ranks complete and feasible quotes first and adds per quote whether the minimums are met, the lines short of them and the premium over the best in percent, plus the cheapest feasible offer per line and a recommendation (the first-ranked quote, reason codes `cheapest_landed`, `complete`, `meets_moq`, `fastest`, `only_offer`, the saving against the next usable offer and the saving of a line-by-line split); a request is awardable from a draft. Web: the session keeps the tenant's edition and `updateSessionTenant` changes it on the spot; `traderNavigation` (Home, Offers, Orders, Suppliers, Customers, Invoices, Receipts, Items, Stock, Rates, Settings) chosen by `navigationFor(edition)` for the top bar (`FlatNav`, the links themselves), the drawer, the shortcuts and the palette; the Edition card under Settings; `/trader/offers` (the list in plain words; a new comparison with what to buy, the suppliers and the items; an offer per supplier with currency, delivery, shipping, charges, and per item price, minimum quantity and note; the comparison run on open and after every offer, the recommendation card with reasons and savings, the offers table with totals in the company's currency, average per unit, minimum-quantity badge, rank and why-not notes, the cheapest supplier per item, "order from this supplier" into a purchase order with a link to it); EN/AR. ## In progress

- **Trader programme** (founder decision 2026-11-02, ROADMAP "Trader programme", ADR-0035, A-186, A-187): a trader edition of the same product for a small import business run by a non-accountant. T1 (the edition setting and switch, the flat trader shell, the Offers screen over requests for quotation with minimum order quantities, notes, the landed comparison, the recommendation with reasons and savings, the cheapest offer per item and ordering from the recommended supplier) T2 (shipments: the order's dated journey, the costs at the rates paid, what was paid for the goods, the landed cost per unit, hints, arrival posting the receipt and the landed costs, the supplier's invoice into the payable) T3 (the pricing board: landed cost at the rate paid, cost to buy again at today's rate, current price and margin, the suggested price at the target margin, alerts, applying into the sales price list; the Trader module, A-188) T4 (the one-step sale, the Sales screen with each invoice's state and payments, the Money screen with aging, chase-first and pay-next and sales by month, A-189) and T5 (the Health screen: the income statement in plain words from the ledger, fixed costs, break-even, the month's pace, cash runway and the money position, warnings, the shipments on the way with their money, sales by item and by customer, what to reorder, A-190) T6 (the Stock screen with the count in three steps and the finish in one call, A-191) and T7 (the trader's home: the day in plain words over the home and health read models, A-192) are done 2026-11-02, T8 (everything linked: paying the supplier and the journey's costs from the shipment as advances and payments that move the books, the advances applied to the invoices, Pay on Money, the bank subledger behind journals, the whole chain in one test, A-193, A-194) and T9 (expenses in one step from the trader's screen through the Expenses module, the usual kinds of spend made once, the owner's capital and drawings as manual journals through the new `IJournals` contract, A-195) are done 2026-11-03: the programme is reported in `docs/reports/trader-programme-report.md`. T10 (the founder's field feedback of 2026-11-03, A-196) is in progress: T10a (the session renewed, the short sale refused plainly, the bar freed) and T10b (the Offers screen rebuilt) are done; next T10c (the day's exchange rate asked for at first open after 08:00), T10d (the pricing board editable, the item's own cost, a floor price), T10e (the next step after a customer or supplier is made), T11a–T11c (the founder's second batch, A-200 to A-202: the sign-out across windows, no tax on unclassified items for the trader, on-hand on Items, any offer ordered from, prices edited with the margin live; the cash and bank screen, newest first, the period; the trader program on its own with zero data, the trader edition from sign-up, the app on the phone's home screen); then the earlier list (returns and discounts from the sales screen, a customer page with a statement, receipts on account, partial arrivals, costs after receipt). Deferred from T9: a payee made from the expense form (the suppliers screen makes one), an expense in a foreign currency from the trader's screen, reversing an owner movement from the trader's screen (the Journals screen reverses it). Deferred from T8: inventory at the advance's rate for the part paid in advance (IFRIC 22; the difference shows as exchange, A-193), a supplier invoice that differs from the order, paying a cost invoice from the shipment after arrival (Money pays it), refunding an advance on a cancelled shipment from the shipment screen (Banking's refund stands). Deferred from T6: a phone shell of its own (the scanner installs as a web app), barcode label printing, a trader-worded mobile home. Deferred from T5: profit by item (needs the cost of each sale from the stock ledger), a cash forecast by week from the open items' due dates, a lead time per supplier in the reorder (one cover-days setting), recording an expense from the health screen (the trader's Expenses screen records one, T9). Deferred from T4: a trader-worded customer page, discounts and returns from the sales screen, receipts on account. Deferred from T3: prices per customer group or quantity break, margins per item or category, a history of suggestions, costs for items never shipped nor stocked. Deferred from T2: costs after receipt (a landed-cost document by hand), partial arrivals, an invoice that differs from the order (paying the supplier from the shipment screen is done in T8). Deferred from T1: a split order placed by the program (the saving of a split is shown, the trader places the second order himself); offers in a foreign currency need a spot rate of the day under Rates (the comparison says which rate is missing); a trader-worded purchase order screen (the full Orders screen opens from "Open the order"); the phone version (a home-screen app since T11c; a native store app needs a Mac with Xcode and an Apple developer account).
- **Founder programme**: items 1 (UI ease), 2 (expenses, 6.4, A-174), 3 (fixed assets and depreciation, 6.6, A-175), 4 (print and send, 5.9, A-176), 5 (cheques and post-dated cheques, 6.3, A-177; bank statement import and reconciliation, 6.2, A-178) 6 (e-invoicing, ZATCA phase 2, 8.6, A-179), 7 (point of sale, offline-capable, A-180, ADR-0032) and 8 (payroll and HR, simple, A-181, ADR-0033) are done; item 9, reports without a programmer (ADR-0034, A-182, A-183), is in progress: slice 9a (the projected facts, the catalogue, the query compiler with explain and drill) and slice 9b (the report builder screen with pivot, charts, drill, the query shown, exports, saved reports and shares) slice 9c (the statement engine, the shipped profit and loss, balance sheet and trial balance tied to the ledger, the statements screen and the designer) and slice 9d (dashboards run as the reader and pinned to the home page; schedules of reports and statements as CSV, XLSX or PDF emailed through the worker with their run history) are done; next 9e ask-your-data. Deferred from item 8: salary advances and loans as a ledger of their own (an advance is recovered through a fixed deduction component), end-of-service benefits, attendance and overtime, income tax brackets, a bank payment file (the payments exist; the file format is a later export), payslip email runs, a payroll register report, a configurable proration basis (thirty days for now), a department picker beyond a dimension whose code contains "DEP". Deferred from item 7: a multi-currency drawer (dollars and dinars at one counter), lot- and serial-tracked items at the till, promotions and quantity breaks applied offline (the till prices at quantity one from the cached catalogue; the server refuses what it cannot reconcile), refunds without a receipt, cash drawer, customer display and receipt-printer integrations beyond the browser's print, a till-side ZATCA signature (the simplified invoice is signed and reported by the server on sync, within the scheme's 24 hours), till-level stock on hand and a session report print. Deferred from item 5: the cheque printing template, cheques from payment proposals, bank feeds through APIs, rules that match a statement line to an open item, an amount tolerance in matching. Deferred from item 6: the certification run against ZATCA's sandbox and simulation portals, which needs the founder's OTP from the Fatoora portal (the simulator holds the implementation to the scheme's rules until then), debit notes from the sales side (the adapter and the compliance samples carry them; no sales document issues one yet), the unit code of a line from its unit of measure (PCE for now), Peppol and PDF/A-3.
- 5.3 tax is done (see "M5 slices"): the engine, purchase documents taxed, prices converted between tax bases, the return, filing and e-invoicing export, and the tax screens with a journey and demo set-up.
- 5.4 quotations, orders, credit control and drop-ship is done (see "M5 slices"): the module, credit-limit hold and override, backordering and retry, the drop-ship purchase-order round trip, the quotations and orders screens with a browser journey, and the demo seed.
- 5.5 pick, pack, ship is done (see "M5 slices"): shipments, lot/serial lines, pick lists with FEFO/bin-sequence planning, the handheld picking screen, packages, carrier details, screens, journeys and demo seed.
- 5.6 invoicing is done (see "M5 slices"): the receivables subledger, invoices from shipments and orders, direct invoices, credit notes with automatic application, deferred revenue, commission accrual, recurring invoices, the e-invoice status, the invoice, recurring-invoice and receivables screens with a journey and the demo invoices.
- 5.7 receivables is done (see "M5 slices"): customer receipts and deposits with realised FX, charges, discount given and withholding; write-offs; promises to pay; dunning levels, runs and letters with fees and credit holds; netting against payables; the receipts screen, the receivables screen's new actions and dunning tab, a journey and the demo. 
- 5.8 returns is done (see "M5 slices"): the RMA flow from request to credit at the shipment's cost with lot recall and the serial repair chain, the returns screen with the serial history on it, a journey and the demo. Next: 5.9 print pipeline (Scriban templates, Chromium rendering in the worker, bilingual invoice, order and statement templates, email with PDF, render cache and evidence copy), then 5.10 demo seed v5.
- 5.9 print pipeline is done (see "M5 slices"): templates and their versions, Chromium renders with the cache and the evidence copy, emails with the PDF, the print dialog on invoices, orders and statements with WhatsApp and share links, the template screen, a journey.

## Post-milestone fixes

| Date | Item |
|------|------|
| 2026-09-22 | **`make demo` / `make up` never actually worked on a truly fresh PostgreSQL cluster.** `MigrationRunner.EnsureDatabaseAndRolesAsync` referenced an Npgsql parameter (`@password`) from inside a dollar-quoted `DO $$ ... $$` block; parameter placeholders are never substituted inside dollar-quoted text, so PostgreSQL parsed the literal `@password` as the `@` operator on a column, failing with `column "password" does not exist" whenever the `quicker_app` role did not already exist. Every environment this project had run in (CI's Postgres service included) happened to already have that role from an earlier run, so the broken branch was always skipped and the bug was invisible until a founder ran it on a brand-new machine. Fixed by handing the password to the session with `set_config()` (a normal parameterized statement) and reading it back with `current_setting()` inside the DO block, verified end to end including a password containing a quote. Also fixed in the same pass: an unused `using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore;` in `ActivityService.cs` that only fails the build on a clean checkout (incremental builds here never re-ran the analyzer on that unchanged file). Both were caught only because a real first run on a clean Windows machine hit them; worth periodically testing `make demo` against a genuinely fresh database and a genuinely fresh checkout, not just an incrementally-updated one. |
| 2026-09-23 | **Dates scrambled in Arabic grids.** `Intl.DateTimeFormat` for Arabic locales embeds right-to-left marks between the date parts; inside the explicit-direction spans the grids use they reversed the parts ("232026/09/"). `formatDate`/`formatDateTime` now strip the marks. Found on a screenshot review of the purchasing screens. |
| 2026-09-23 | **Settlement kinds shown as raw codes** on the invoice's payables tab (`credit_application`); every kind now has an English and Arabic label (`purchasing.settlementKinds`). |
| 2026-09-23 | **The demo seed test still expected 8 invariant checks** after 4.3 added a ninth (and 4.7 two more), so it had been failing unnoticed (it takes about six minutes and was not in the per-slice runs). It now asserts the registered list (`InvariantCodes.All`), so a new check cannot silently break it again. |
| 2026-09-23 | **`make demo` / `make up` failed on a fresh database after 4.7.** The 4.7 change to the demo books opened payable open items for the v2 suppliers, which exist only as fixed ids (no partner rows), and the open items' partner foreign key refused them. The seed now creates those five suppliers as partner records with the same ids before posting the books. Found when the migrator ran the demo seed during 4.8; the 4.7 run had not shown the error. |
| 2026-09-23 | **Warning badges failed WCAG AA contrast** (4.42:1 for `--q-warning` #a16207 on its soft background) once a grid showed two "Partly settled" badges under axe; the token is now #854d0e (6.9:1), which also lifts the warning button's white text. |
| 2026-09-23 | **Stock balances ordered non-deterministically for serials created in the same instant.** `GET /api/v1/inventory/stock/balances` ordered rows by lot and serial ids (UUIDv7: same-millisecond ids fall in random order), which showed up as a serial-history test failing only under load. Rows now order by lot number and serial number. Found while running the whole suite with the e2e in parallel during 4.4. |
| 2026-09-23 | **A second company could not number receipts, supplier invoices, returns, landed costs, payments or payment proposals.** Those six services ensured their default numbering series with a bare code (`GRN`, `PI`, `RTN`, `LC`, `PAY`, `PP`), but series codes are unique in the tenant, so the first company took the code and every other company was left with no series (`series.none_applicable` on its first document). Every test used a single company, so it went unseen until the demo seed v4 ran procure-to-pay in all three demo companies. `EnsureDefaultSeriesAsync` now appends the company code (`GRN-IQT`), as orders, RFQs and journals already did; document numbers are unchanged (`GRN-2026-00001`), and series already created keep working because the lookup is by document type and company. Regression test: `A_default_series_is_ensured_for_every_company_of_the_tenant_not_only_the_first`. |
| 2026-09-23 | **Cost adjustment runs showed a raw item id and raw trigger codes** on the valuation screen (found on the M3 screenshot review). Listed runs now carry the item code (`CostAdjustmentRunInfo.ItemCode`, additive) and the trigger and source document read as words in English and Arabic (`inventory.valuation.triggerKinds`, `.sourceDocuments`). |
| 2026-09-23 | **Lots past their expiry date showed "Active"** until the daily `inventory.lots.expire` job ran (it had not in a freshly seeded demo). The stock rules already block such a lot by date; the lots screen and lot detail now show it as expired by date the same way, and the demo seed runs the expiry sweep before it commits, as the daily job would have. |
| 2026-09-23 | **The demo's replenishment planner had nothing to suggest**: the v3 seed set every reorder point at the bottom of the family's opening range, so almost no row started below it (the code comment claimed the opposite). The reorder point now sits a third of the way into the range and the seed runs the planner once per company, so the screen opens on suggestions; the demo test asserts more than 50 open suggestions. |
| 2026-09-23 | **The demo's transfer and count screens were empty.** `DemoOperations` adds, in the trading company, a Basra-to-Erbil transfer received two days after shipping, a second one in transit, a posted count of the Basra beverages with one counting difference and its reason, and a frozen full count of Erbil waiting for the counters (A-115). |
| 2026-09-23 | **The replenishment "why" showed a raw key and code** (`qualityHold`, `reorder_point`) and the cost-run source used guessed document codes; both now read as words in English and Arabic (`inventory.replenishment.why.qualityHold`, `.why.triggers`, `stock_*` source documents). Found on the screenshots taken after the fixes above. |
| 2026-09-23 | **Setting a new standard cost failed with a server error when stock had moved on or after its effective date.** After saving the new version, the service wrote the revaluation run's id back onto the version row, but standard-cost versions are append-only and the application role may not update them; the backend test never reached that path because its second standard had no later movements. The run id is now returned with the new version and derived from the run's trigger when versions are listed; nothing is written back and no figure changes. Regression test `A_new_standard_on_a_day_that_already_has_movements_re_applies_them_and_names_its_run`. Found by the costing journey of the new item-cost screen. This touches the costing service's bookkeeping, not its arithmetic; flagged for the costing design pass to review. |
| 2026-09-23 | **The sign-in policy accepted values that lock a workspace.** `PUT /tenant/policy` checked only the password, token and session ranges: a step-up window of 0 would have refused every sensitive action (including changing the policy back), a lockout threshold of 0 or 1 locked members on their first typo, and password sign-in could be turned off with no single sign-on connection, locking everyone out. The window must now be 1–240 minutes, the threshold 3–100 attempts and the lockout 1–1,440 minutes (`tenant.policy_invalid`); password sign-in turns off only while a connection is active (`tenant.password_login_required`), and that last connection can then be neither disabled nor deleted (`sso.connection_required`). Found while building the sign-in policy tab. Test `Password_sign_in_turns_off_only_while_a_connection_is_active_and_that_connection_cannot_then_be_removed`. |
| 2026-09-23 | **Two default segregation-of-duties rules could never trip.** "Creating suppliers and paying them" named `payables.payment.post`, but supplier payments are released with `banking.payment.post` since 4.7; "approving purchase orders and posting supplier invoices" named `purchasing.order.approve`, which does not exist because order approval is a workflow step, not a permission. So the main vendor-fraud control did nothing. The defaults now pair `partners.supplier.manage` with `banking.payment.post` (block) and `purchasing.order.manage` with `purchasing.invoice.post` (warn, "raising orders and posting invoices", A-118); migration V0032 corrects the system rules of existing tenants (73 locally, tenant by tenant under row-level security) unless a tenant already added the pair itself. Test `Default_sod_rules_name_permissions_that_exist_once_their_module_has_shipped` fails when a rule names a key its shipped module does not register (the sales and receivables rules wait for M5). Found by the access journey. |
| 2026-09-23 | **Role templates could not be saved as roles, and seeded system roles could not be edited.** The templates (and the system roles created from them at sign-up) grant areas of modules still to come (`finance.*`, `tax.*`, `reporting.*`, `sales.*`), which the role API refuses as unknown keys, so "start from a template" and any edit of the accountant, AR or sales roles failed with `role.grant_unknown`. `GET /meta/role-templates` now answers typed, with the grants that exist today apart from the pending ones, and editing a role keeps the grants it already holds while newly added grants must still exist. Tests `Every_role_template_saves_as_a_role_as_it_stands` and `A_system_role_keeps_its_reserved_grants_when_edited_but_new_grants_must_exist`. |
| 2026-09-23 | **CI went red on both server jobs.** (1) The `minio/minio` image was withdrawn from Docker Hub when upstream MinIO stopped publishing community builds, so the .NET job failed at `docker run` before building, and `make up` would have failed on any machine without a cached copy; CI and Compose now use the maintained drop-in fork `pgsty/minio`, pinned to a release (A-122), and the CI step fails if the store does not come up. (2) The browser job starts the API alone, without the worker, so nothing published the ~6,200 events the demo seed writes; once the seed took longer than the 300-second outbox-lag limit (343 s on the runner), `/health/ready` stayed 503 and the job stopped before any journey ran. The API there now runs the dispatcher embedded (`Quicker:Worker:Embedded`, the single-node mode of ADR-0010), so readiness reports a real, draining outbox (locally: all 6,237 events published in about 25 s, none dead). The Postgres service health check names its database, which removes a failed-connection log line every five seconds. With the dispatcher running beside the journeys, the purchasing journey (ten documents, twenty accessibility scans; 45 s alone) went past the one-minute default in a full parallel run, so it has the two-minute budget the costing and payables journeys already had; all 21 journeys pass. |
| 2026-09-23 | **Test projects could clone a half-built template database on a fresh cluster.** Each test project runs as its own process; the first one to find the migration template missing created it and then migrated and seeded it, while the others saw that it already existed and cloned it midway, so their databases lacked tables (`control.users does not exist`) or reference data (`company.currency_unknown` for IQD). It never showed locally, where the template already exists, but failed 29 tests on CI's fresh database. Template builds now take a cluster-wide advisory lock, and a template counts only once it is marked as one (its last step), so a build that died halfway is rebuilt rather than cloned; reproduced with three projects in parallel on a fresh cluster (10 failures before, none after). The same CI run showed two long browser journeys (accounting, inventory) running past the one-minute default on the shared runner and the first journey's sign-up outlasting a five-second wait on a cold API; those journeys have the two-minute budget of the other long ones, and every sign-up waits up to 20 s as the newer journeys already did. |
| 2026-09-23 | **A serial's history could list events out of order.** It sorted by time, then by id, but one stock posting writes several events in the same instant (a transfer ships a serial out of one warehouse and into transit), and ids made in the same millisecond are not ordered, so the serial-history test failed on CI now and then. A first fix added an insertion sequence (`V0033__inventory_serial_event_order.sql`) and read the history in that order, but EF Core writes a batch's rows in key order, not in the order they were added, so the sequence was as random as the ids and CI failed again. The history now reads in time order, then by posting (in the order postings were written), and within one posting what left a warehouse before what arrived; eight runs in a row pass. The sequence column stays for the ordering between postings. |
| 2026-09-24 | **Roles granted a whole area did not see its screens.** The web app decided what to show by comparing permission keys exactly, so a role granted `partners.supplier.*` or `inventory.*` (as the purchaser and inventory-manager templates are) had the navigation entries, palette results and buttons of that area hidden even though the API let it in. The web app now reads grants as the server does (`covers` in `lib/permissions.ts`: `*`, exact keys and `area.*`), with a unit test. Found while giving the sales rep template its screens. |
| 2026-09-24 | **A table wider than its column could not be scrolled from the keyboard** when nothing inside it was focusable (axe `scrollable-region-focusable`, WCAG 2.1.1). The shared table takes focus while it overflows. Found by the sales journey on the Customer 360 overview. |
| 2026-09-24 | **Forms and reports defaulted to yesterday late in the evening.** The web app took "today" as the UTC date, while the API dates documents in the company's time zone, so from 21:00 UTC a Baghdad user's report periods ended the day before that day's documents (the purchasing journey's analysis came up empty when run then). "Today" is now the calendar date in the signed-in person's time zone (their profile), else the browser's. | `apps/web/src/lib/dates.ts` (+ test), `today()` in `routes/accounting/shared.tsx`, the rate form |
| 2026-09-25 | **Table action columns were announced by their key.** Six screens labelled their actions column for screen readers with `common.actions`, a key missing from both language files, so a screen reader read out the key. The key is now in English and Arabic. Found while building the pricing screens. | `apps/web/src/i18n/en.json`, `ar.json` |
| 2026-09-24 | **A workflow test failed from 24 September on.** It took "today" from the wall clock while the test server runs on the fixture's fixed clock (22 September), so once the real date was two days later its delegation (valid from yesterday) had not started by the server's date; it now takes the date from the fixture's clock. No other test mixes the two. |
| 2026-09-30 | **A sales order could not be confirmed for a warehouse with bins.** A reservation had to name a bin in such a warehouse (`reservation.bin_required`), but an order reserves at warehouse level and leaves the shelves to picking, so confirmation failed for every warehouse with bins (the demo only ever sold from the Basra warehouse, which has none). A reservation without a bin is now checked against every bin's stock and held on the warehouse's bin-less balance row, and the stock engine releases it from that row when the shipment takes the stock from a bin (the invariant harness already expected the hold there). Found while building 5.5b's bin-sequence picking; covered by `PickingTests`. |
| 2026-09-30 | **The journal screen did not say when a journal was out of balance.** Reported by the founder: a journal with unequal debits and credits appeared to go through. The server has always refused to submit or post an unbalanced manual, accrual or allocation journal (`journal.unbalanced`, then the engine's `posting.unbalanced`), and an unbalanced draft may be saved as work in progress; an **opening** journal is the deliberate exception, posted with its difference on opening balance equity (2.3). But the editor showed only the two totals, the Post and Submit buttons looked the same either way, and nothing said where an opening journal's difference would go, so an opening journal or a saved draft looked like an unbalanced posting. The editor and the journal now show the state under the totals (balanced; out of balance by the amount, with "save as a draft only"; or, for an opening journal, the difference going to opening balance equity), and Submit and Post are disabled until the journal balances. The server had no test of an unbalanced journal through the journal API (only through the engine); `An_unbalanced_journal_saves_as_a_draft_but_is_never_submitted_or_posted` now covers submit and post refused with both sides named, nothing in the ledger, then the same journal balanced and posted. Also a unit test of the balance rule (`lib/journalBalance.test.ts`, exact decimals) and the accounting journey drafts out of balance, sees the warning and disabled buttons, balances and posts. | `apps/web/src/routes/accounting/JournalsPage.tsx`, `apps/web/src/lib/journalBalance.ts`, `JournalTests.cs`, `accounting-journeys.spec.ts` |
| 2026-10-01 | **Currencies had to be typed.** Founder request: every currency field (30 across journals, accounts, items, pricing, quotations, orders, opportunities, customers, suppliers, bank accounts, payments, proposals, agreements, RFQ quotes, landed costs, purchase orders, supplier invoices, purchasing and sales settings, and the company form) is now a searchable picker (`routes/CurrencyField.tsx`, an ARIA combobox): the favourites first (IQD, USD, AED by default), then every active currency, searched by code or English/Arabic name; a full code typed or pasted still chooses it. The favourites are a workspace setting with an editor under Settings → Workspace (A-155). Escape in an open list inside a dialog now closes the list only, not the dialog (`@quicker/ui` `DialogContent`). Tests: unit tests of the options and the setting (`lib/currencies.test.ts`); the accounting journey checks the favourites first, a search by name, Escape, and choosing by click, with axe on the open list. | `apps/web/src/routes/CurrencyField.tsx`, `apps/web/src/lib/currencies.ts`, `SettingsPage.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/components/dialog.tsx` |
| 2026-10-01 | **Journal description and trial balance grouping** (founder requests). (1) The journal editor has one Description field in any language (or both) instead of an English and an Arabic one; the text is stored for every language so every screen and export shows it. (2) The trial balance groups by 19 journal groupings besides the dimensions: partner, who posted, day, week, month, quarter, year, fiscal period, document type, module, journal entry, source document, entry kind, transaction currency, branch, subledger, tax code, posting role, due month (A-156); records read by code and name; every grouped row drills to the ledger narrowed to its group (`g.KEY` filters), which closes on the figure. Tests: `The_trial_balance_groups_by_any_grouping_and_every_group_drills_to_exactly_its_lines` (20 groupings, each balanced, adding up to the ungrouped report, every row drilled), the architecture test with the two new contract dependencies, and the accounting journey grouping by who posted and by month. | `JournalGroupings.cs`, `InquiryService.cs`, `TrialBalancePage.tsx`, `JournalsPage.tsx`, `InquiryTests.cs` |
| 2026-10-01 | **Cost centres where lines are entered** (founder request). The journal editor and journal view show the cost centre as a column next to the account (other dimensions stay in the line details; an account that blocks the cost centre disables it). Requisition and purchase order lines take their dimensions by value (`dimensions: {"COST_CENTER": id}`, as supplier invoice lines already did; `requisition.dimensions_ambiguous` / `order.dimensions_ambiguous` when a set is given too) and return them; the order raised from a requisition, and the supplier invoice against the order, inherit them, so the expense reaches the journal on the cost centre. The purchasing line editor and line tables show a Cost centre column on requisitions and orders. Tests: the requisition-to-order test and the invoice-dimensions test, the purchasing journey charging a requisition line to a cost centre and seeing it on the order, the journal-lines journey. | `JournalLineDetails.tsx`, `purchasing/shared.tsx`, `RequisitionService.cs`, `PurchaseOrderService.cs` |
| 2026-10-01 | **Cost centres on sales documents** (founder request, A-157). Migration `V0052`: `dimension_set_id` on quotation, order and shipment lines and on the stock ledger and value entries. Quotation and order lines take `dimensions` by value and return them; a converted order and a shipment inherit the line's set; the shipment's stock movement keeps it and the costing engine books it on the cost of goods sold (not on inventory), as do later cost adjustments and the reversal. The sales line editor and line tables show a Cost centre column. Tests: `A_line_s_cost_centre_travels_from_quotation_to_order_to_shipment_and_lands_on_cost_of_goods_sold_only` (quotation → order → shipment, the trial balance grouped by cost centre shows only cost of goods sold on it, the reversal clears it, a value of another dimension refused), the sales journey charging a quotation line and seeing it on the order. | `V0052__sales_and_stock_dimensions.sql`, `QuotationService.cs`, `OrderService.cs`, `ShipmentService.cs`, `CostingService.cs`, `sales/shared.tsx` |
| 2026-10-30 | **Journal entry review** (founder request: accounting and UI/UX). Accounting (A-158): submit and post check what posting would refuse (dimension rules, manual posting, currency restriction, control accounts, zero-amount lines) with the line number, so nothing is approved that cannot post; the preparer of a journal cannot approve it; an accrual reverses after its posting date whatever the request's flag. Screens: errors in the journal editor were swallowed whenever the shared error mapping named a field the form does not have (a required dimension, a blocked account, "at least one line") — they now always show, and the refused line is highlighted; a line with an amount but no account was silently dropped on saving — it is now refused with its number; the account is picked from the chart (by code or name) and an unknown, header, inactive or documents-only account is flagged as it is typed; a new line starts with the amount that balances the journal; amounts show the currency's decimals (3 for IQD, KWD, JOD) instead of always two; line descriptions are one text in any language like the header; "Cancel" (which cancelled the journal at once and read like "close") is "Cancel journal" with a confirmation; Submit shows only when the company requires approval ("Submit for approval"), otherwise a draft posts directly; Reject needs its reason and Correct asks why before drafting the correction; a posted journal shows its currency, reference, reversal date and, once reversed, a link to the reversing entry (Correct is then hidden); messages and reasons no longer carry over to the next journal opened. Journal entries: totals row, line descriptions, the empty side blank instead of 0.00, the functional columns only for foreign-currency entries, currency decimals. Tests: `What_posting_would_refuse_is_refused_at_submit_and_the_preparer_cannot_approve`, the accounting journey (unknown account flagged, the balancing new line, an account-less line refused, cancel confirmation). | `ManualJournalService.cs`, `JournalsPage.tsx`, `JournalLineDetails.tsx`, `JournalBrowserPage.tsx`, `lib/problem.ts` |
| 2026-11-03 | **Opening cash missing from the cash box** (found while checking that the trader's chain is linked end to end, A-194). A journal line on a bank or cash account (the setup wizard's opening balances, manual and recurring journals, the demo books) reached the ledger but recorded no movement in the box it named, so the box's balance on the bank, Money, Health and Home screens left out the opening cash while the trial balance had it; the bank invariant could not see it because it only followed lines pointing at a bank transaction. Banking now follows journals' bank lines like Payables and Receivables follow theirs (refusing a line that names no box, another account's box or a box in another currency), the invariant compares every box with all its ledger lines, migration V0075 records the movements of the lines already posted, and the demo books open a real operating account and cash box. Tests: `BankJournalTests`, the setup wizard's opening cash checked in the box, the trader chain test. |
| 2026-11-03 | **Signed out after ten minutes, and "Unauthorized" on the next click** (founder's first day on the trader edition). The web app never renewed its access token: the token lasts ten minutes (the workspace policy), the session fourteen days, and the client signed out on the first 401 instead of trading the refresh token for new tokens. Now a 401 renews the session once for every request that met it and sends each request again with the new token; only a session that is itself expired or revoked signs the person out. Found with it: the sale in one step refused a sale of more than the store holds with the shipment's technical wording ("exceeds what the order line has reserved"); it now says, in the trader's words and in Arabic, what is on hand and what was wanted (`quick_sale.not_enough_stock`), before anything is written. The workspace name no longer sits in the top bar (it stays in the account menu), to make room on a small screen. Tests: `renewal.test.ts` (the renewal and the sign-out), `QuickSaleTests` (the refusal's facts), the trader journey trying to sell a thousand pads. |

## Next

0. **Founder programme, 2026-10-31** (ROADMAP "Founder programme", A-166): thirteen items in order, starting with UI ease (home dashboard, status bars, quick create, smart defaults, keyboard lines, saved views with group-by, setup wizard and imports), then expenses, then fixed assets, then the market features. The current item is named in "In progress".
1. **Founder decision, 2026-09-24:** start M5 (order to cash) now, built to be the best on the market ("nothing but a masterpiece"); this lifts the reservation of item 2 for M5. Order of work: 5.1 customer master and CRM (done), 5.2 pricing (done), 5.3 tax (done), 5.4 quotes and orders with credit control (done), 5.5 shipping (done), then 5.6 invoicing, 5.7 receivables, 5.8 returns, 5.9 print pipeline, 5.10 demo seed v5.
2. **Division of work (founder decision, 2026-09-23):** until further notice the routine, additive slices (reports and inquiries, demo seeds, screens over existing services, fixes) are built without changing the foundations laid so far (posting engine, costing, payables and banking settlement mechanics, invariants). The design-heavy work is reserved for a later, more capable pass: the order-to-cash design (M5: sales documents, pricing engine, receivables with realised FX), tax engine and returns, FX revaluation with auto-reversal (6.5), financial statements and the layout engine (6.10), consolidation and intercompany (M6), and any change to ADRs 0006/0017/0031. **Founder decision, 2026-09-24:** the reservation is lifted for the known defects of M3–M4 so they are fixed now rather than in the design pass: open issues I1–I4, invoice-line dimensions reaching the journal, a receipt line invoiced at two prices, and the harness after the browser journeys. The rest of the reservation stands.
2. Open founder question Q9 (Iraq statutory code list) can be answered at any time; nothing built depends on it.

## Known gaps and interim pieces (explicit, per the working rules)

- **Payables and banking (4.7) boundaries** (ASSUMPTIONS A-113): netting with receivables waits for the receivables module (5.7); a payment goes out of an account in the payment currency or in the company's currency (a third currency waits for the cross-currency transfer mechanics of 6.1); a cheque is a payment method only (cheque books, post-dated cheques and their events are 6.2); bank statements, reconciliation, transfers, charges and interest as documents of their own are 6.1, so a bank account's balance today is the sum of its payment movements; write-offs and revaluation of open items are 6.5 (the settlement kinds exist, nothing creates them yet); a proposal is executed as draft payments per supplier to be posted one by one (no bank file or batch approval yet); withholding at payment is computed on the gross amount of each line by the supplier's code (no threshold logic at payment, the invoice-time threshold stays as built in 4.4); company bank account numbers are stored in clear and masked on display (the partner bank accounts of 4.1 are encrypted; the same protection moves here with 6.1); the bank invariant compares each account's control lines with its transactions only where a line references a transaction, because the demo books and manual journals still move bank accounts without one until 6.1 makes every bank movement a transaction (the payables invariant, by contrast, sums every AP line, which is why the demo seed now opens and settles real open items behind its supplier journals).
- **Returns and debit notes (4.6) boundaries** (ASSUMPTIONS A-112): supplier advances (payment on account) moved from 4.6 to 4.7, where bank and cash accounts arrive: an advance is a bank-side posting and its open item settles invoices the same way a debit note does (`ap_settlements` already carries the `advance_application` kind). A return does not touch the order's quantities (the order was fulfilled; a replacement is a new order or a new receipt against the open line), and it does not lower what the receipt can still be invoiced for beyond the returned quantity; the supplier's credit comes as a debit note. A debit note credits returned goods at the returned cost and books what the supplier credits above or below it as purchase price variance, in the note's currency at the note's rate; where the note is in a foreign currency, the GRNI credit in the company's currency can differ from the return's cost by a rounding unit of that currency, which then stays under the return's reference (the invariant accounts for it) until 4.7's revaluation and write-off tools clear such residues. Credit application is same-currency only (cross-currency application waits for the payment engine of 4.7, which brings the rate handling); a settlement is not reversed on its own yet — reverse the debit note after unapplying, which 4.7 adds together with payment reversal.
- **Landed costs (4.5) boundaries** (ASSUMPTIONS A-111): a document lands on receipt lines of one company and is allocated in the company's currency at the posting-date rate; charges entered straight from a supplier invoice without an estimate (POSTING_RULES "Cr AP when entered from the charge invoice") go through a document first (draft, post, then the charge line settles it), which keeps one allocation path; the sold portion is what the costing engine's adjustment run re-applied to consumption (for average costing that follows the engine's re-application, not a per-layer split); weight and volume come from the item master (per base unit), not from the receipt; a settled document is reversed by reversing the charge invoice first; the allocation report is the document view (a company-wide report by period joins the reporting work of M8).
- **Supplier invoices (4.4) boundaries** (ASSUMPTIONS A-110): tolerance rules are the supplier account's effective price and quantity tolerances (no separate rule table by category yet; `pur_tolerance_rules` of the domain model waits for a need); tax lines are zero until the tax engine (M5) prices them and the input-tax line joins the journal; prepayment schedules (deferrals) and capitalisation to assets (M7) are not on the invoice yet; a matched line is invoiced in the order's currency; the payables view is the open-item list (aging, proposals and payments are 4.7); the invoice's approval workflow and its blocks are separate definitions (on-submit and on-block), as the engine defines them.
- **Goods receipts (4.3) boundaries** (ASSUMPTIONS A-109): services and expense items are not received on a goods receipt (they are settled by the supplier invoice, 4.4); a receipt is reversed as a whole, partial returns are the supplier-return document of 4.6; quality hold on receipt is not a separate state yet (the stock engine's quality-hold bins from 3.5 can be named on the line); mobile receiving posts at once and needs a connection (it does not join the offline queue of 3.8); bins, lots and serials are typed on the line rather than scanned from the receiving screen's list; drop-ship receipts (POSTING_RULES) wait for the sales module.
- **Purchasing (4.2) boundaries** (ASSUMPTIONS A-108): purchase-order tax comes from the tax engine since 5.3b (A-147); commitments are recorded on approval and released on cancel, but budget control (`budget_exceeded` blocks and overrides) waits for M6, as does consuming commitments at invoice; the purchase-order email carries a bilingual HTML body, the PDF attachment comes with the print pipeline (5.9); supplier quotes are recorded by the buyer (the supplier portal is M8); quote comparison converts at today's spot rate with no landed-cost model beyond freight and other charges; requisition lines without an item are ordered by hand from the order screen; the web line editor takes item codes (with suggestions) and unit codes. (Receipts, invoices, landed costs and returns followed in 4.3–4.6 and keep an order's received and invoiced quantities.)
- **Tax engine (5.3) boundaries** (ASSUMPTIONS A-146, A-147, A-149, A-150): the engine, templates, ledger, return report, filing, the generic UBL 2.1 export, the clearance interface and the setup and returns screens exist; purchase orders and supplier invoices tax their lines through it since 5.3b, and sales documents will as 5.4 and 5.6 build them; supplier returns and debit notes credit tax at the debit note's date and rate rather than the original invoice's; manual journal lines naming a tax code write no ledger entry yet, deliberately deferred past 5.3c (a manual journal line carries no direction or recoverability, which the entry needs, and would make Accounting and Tax depend on each other for a case few tenants hit; revisit if a real filing needs it); no ZATCA, Peppol or ETA adapter ships yet, so a regime naming a scheme is refused (`tax.clearance_not_configured`) rather than cleared, and clearance submissions are not persisted (no `tax_einvoice_submissions` row is written; `ClearanceService` calls the registered provider and returns its answer, nothing more); the returns screen has no UBL export or clearance-submit action yet (there is no document screen to hang it from before 5.6's invoices; the API already serves both); registrations are per company, not per branch; the regime's tax point is recorded but every document is taxed on its own date (cash-basis schemes wait for a regime that needs them); excise and withholding kinds exist on codes, withholding itself stays with the partners' WHT codes of M4 and has no certificate report yet; the templates' rates and boxes were checked against the authorities' published returns as of 2026 and carry a version, and a template changed later is not re-applied to a regime already installed; the demo tenant's live month of purchasing (4.9) predates the demo's tax registrations (5.3d runs after it), so its return preview is legitimately empty rather than seeded with fabricated postings.
- **Customer master and CRM (5.1) boundaries** (ASSUMPTIONS A-142, A-143): customer accounts and groups carry no price list yet (the pricing engine of 5.2 adds it with its tables); the account's tax group is checked against the tax module since 5.3a; credit limits, exposure basis, overdue blocks and credit holds are stored and read by `ICustomerDirectory` (a block already refuses through `EnsureCustomerAsync`), but measuring exposure against the limit and holding orders is the credit check of 5.4; `dunning_level` is set by the dunning runs of 5.7; commission plans quote what a sale earns, and the accruals are booked by invoicing (5.6); lost reasons are free text (no configurable list); pipeline totals are per currency, not converted to one; activities have no reminders or calendar invitations yet (the notification scheduler of M8). Lists answer the first 500 rows and the board the first 2,000 deals (won and lost columns the last 90 days) until the saved views and paging of the grid reach these screens; opportunities are never deleted (a mistaken one is moved to lost, so the pipeline's history stays whole); the Customer 360 shows payables' balances and documents today, and sales documents and receivables join it as 5.4–5.7 implement the same contract.
- **Invoicing and receivables (5.6a) boundaries** (ASSUMPTIONS A-159): a direct invoice takes non-stock items and service lines only; a stock item is invoiced from its shipment (`invoice.stock_item_needs_shipment`) until direct stock invoices move the stock with the invoice; an invoice mixing items priced with tax included and lines whose amounts are net is refused (`invoice.mixed_tax_basis`); invoice quantities against shipment and order lines are entered in the item's base unit (like the fulfilment quantities, A-152); a credit note's lines default to `Revenue` (the `SalesReturns` role waits for returns, 5.8); credit notes are applied to the invoice they name only when they name one (a free credit note stays on account for 5.7's receipts and applications); an invoice's instalments follow the customer's payment terms from the document date, with no delivery-date basis; there is no approval workflow on invoices (posting is the `sales.invoice.post` permission; the journal-style approval setting is a candidate once a tenant asks); deferred revenue runs in calendar months from the start date (no daily method on invoices, though Accounting's engine has one) and a line once partly recognised makes the invoice creditable, not reversible; commissions accrue at invoice only (plans accruing at payment or on collected amounts wait for 5.7's receipts), a plan's tiers accumulate over the calendar month, quarter or year of the posting date rather than the fiscal period, and a margin basis takes a shipped line's cost of goods sold at posting (a later cost adjustment of the shipment does not re-quote the commission); recurring invoices bill items and service lines only and carry no price list of their own; the e-invoice status is `pending` under a scheme until an adapter answers — no ZATCA, Peppol or ETA adapter ships yet, so every submission is refused with `tax.clearance_not_configured` and the UBL export remains the document's record; the receivables side has no receipts, write-offs, dunning or revaluation yet (5.7: `receivables.writeoff.post` is forward-declared only); `ar_open_items.dunning_level`/`promised_payment_date` are stored and read, never set; an order's own status does not say "invoiced" (its lines' `qty_invoiced` does); the invoice screen has no print or email yet (the print pipeline is 5.9) and the e-invoice panel's submit is refused until an adapter ships; the receivables screen takes no receipts yet (5.7's receipts and allocations), only holds and the application of credits and deposits already on account; the dashboard carries no receivables tile yet.
- **Receivables (5.7) boundaries** (ASSUMPTIONS A-161, A-162): receipts are by transfer, cash or cheque recorded at once; post-dated cheques with their deposit, clearing and bounce life cycle (hard scenario 10) are 6.x's cheque book, so a bounced cheque today is a reversed receipt; a receipt refunds nothing (an overpayment stays on account for the next invoice; a refund document waits for 5.9's print pipeline and 6.x's payment runs); customer accounts carry no standing withholding code (the code is named per receipt); write-offs are by hand per item (no threshold-driven run); dunning letters are data, rendered by nothing yet (the print pipeline is 5.9), `sent_at` is set by hand, fees carry no tax and a level never releases a credit hold it placed; netting is one receivable against one payable in one currency (no agreements, schedules or cross-currency netting); unrealised revaluation of open items is 6.5; collections worklists, dunning by e-mail and promises that escalate automatically are not built.
- **Returns (5.8a) boundaries** (ASSUMPTIONS A-163): a return's quarantine disposition is enforced by the engine only for a blocked lot (its stock on hold) or through the bin it is put away in (the pick planner skips quarantine and returns bins); an untracked item quarantined in a warehouse without bins is recorded, not held; the repair of a returned serial ends through the serial's own status change (no repair order); a return of a recalled lot cannot be reversed (the stock engine refuses to ship a blocked lot, which is right); `current` cost on a return is the costing engine's expected cost at that date; returns of drop-shipped lines and the onward return of customer-returned goods to a supplier are not modelled (the purchasing return serves the latter); a return of several shipments is requested line by line on the screen (one request may span a customer's shipments), while "Return goods" on a shipment pre-fills that shipment only.
- **Sales quotations (5.4a) boundaries** (ASSUMPTIONS A-151): a quotation has no PDF or email (the print pipeline is 5.9); pricing and tax re-run on every save of a draft (correct, since a draft is still mutable) but there is no explicit "re-price" action separate from editing a line; opportunity linkage (`opportunity_id`) is accepted but nothing yet reads or writes it from the CRM side; discount and promotion usage from a quotation's pricing is not yet recorded against `IPricing.RecordPromotionUsageAsync` (that happens once an order is confirmed, in 5.4b, so a quotation that is never accepted never consumes a limited promotion); `SalesPermissions.InvoicePost` is still forward-declared only (checked by no endpoint until 5.6) so the default SoD rule naming it does not go silently dead now that `sales.*` has shipped; `.OrderManage` is no longer forward-declared — 5.4b wires it to real endpoints.
- **Sales orders, credit control and drop-ship (5.4b, 5.4c) boundaries** (ASSUMPTIONS A-152): the generic pre-confirm approval step other documents have (`on_submit`) is not wired for orders, only the credit-limit block; exposure nets only the customer's orders in the *same currency* as the one being confirmed (no cross-currency consolidation) and compares against `CreditLimit` naively, though `SaveCustomerAccountRequest` documents that field as the company's *functional* currency — correct only when a company's functional currency, its customers' account currency and its orders' currency all agree, as the demo and this slice's tests deliberately keep them; the `open_ar` exposure basis alone never blocks anything until Receivables (5.7) supplies the other half; `overdue_block_days` and a manual hold reason are stored on the customer account (5.1) but nothing yet reads them (only `limit_exceeded` blocks, matching hard scenario 6 exactly) — overdue blocking needs posted AR (5.7) to mean anything; a warehouse with bins enabled cannot auto-reserve from order confirmation (`IStockReservations.ReserveAsync` requires a bin the order never names), so such a non-drop-ship line always backorders until reserved by hand from the existing Inventory reservations screen; `promotionCode` is not resolved for display on order lines (`promotionId` is).
- **Supplier master (4.1) boundaries** (ASSUMPTIONS A-107): the customer side of the partner arrived with 5.1 (customer accounts, credit, sales reps and the CRM) and price lists come with 5.2; withholding codes live in `ptr_wht_codes` until the tax engine (M5) applies them at invoice or payment, when they become `tax_codes` of kind `withholding`; `tax_group_id` on the supplier account is a plain uuid until then; supplier portal users and relationships (affiliate, parent) beyond the parent link are M8; the item master's supplier references (`itm_item_suppliers.partner_id`) are still plain uuids and the demo seed does not yet create partners (both with the purchasing seed of 4.9); no CSV import/export of partners yet (the import toolkit of M8); performance scores are 4.8; the web screens create and edit but do not delete contacts, addresses, bank accounts or registrations (the API does).
- **Workflow core (4.0) boundaries** (ASSUMPTIONS A-106): approvers are named members or a role; the manager chain and dynamic owners (cost-centre owner, category owner, account manager) wait for positions and those masters; `on_post_attempt` and `on_field_change` are accepted triggers with no caller yet (documents run `on_submit`; the posting-time re-run and field-change re-approval arrive with purchasing documents); the reapproval policy is stored, not yet enforced (no subject re-evaluates after an approval); `require_step_up` is stored, not enforced (step-up authentication lands with the identity pass of M4/M5); email approval links, one-click approve, bulk approve and the mobile inbox are 8.1; stock adjustments are the only real subject so far (journals keep their company-setting approval until they move to the engine), and counts, transfers and assemblies raise no blocks yet (negative stock, price floor and credit limit blocks come with the documents that hit them); the web definitions editor writes conditions as text with a checker (the visual rule builder is 8.1) and picks escalation by role only (members through the API).

- **Item references to modules that do not exist yet are plain uuids** (ASSUMPTIONS A-096): supplier `partner_id` (partners, M4/M5), `default_bin_id` in warehouse settings (warehouses themselves are real foreign keys since 3.2), `image_attachment_id` (an attachment uploaded through `/collaboration/attachments` with `entityType: item`; existence is not checked). Items have no costing override of their own: the category's applies, then the company's method.
- **M3 web pass boundaries**: the item screens edit units, barcodes, suppliers, per-warehouse planning and per-company costing settings since the post-M4 routine pass, and, since the same pass, bills of material, variants (with the attributes they combine), substitutes and the item picture; reservations and inbound cost adjustments have no screen by design (A-116); standard costs, revaluations and the per-entry cost explanation have screens since the post-M4 routine pass; document lines name items by code with suggestions by code or name; transfers ship in part and receive with shortages since the post-M4 routine pass; the ⌘K search covers companies, items and suppliers (documents by number are not searchable yet).
- **Demo seed v4 (4.9) boundaries** (ASSUMPTIONS A-115): the demo's bank accounts show negative balances, because payments go out of them and nothing comes in: deposits, transfers between accounts and bank opening balances are slice 6.1, and customer receipts 5.7; the months before the current one hold purchase orders only (closed, not posted), because the demo books hard-close them and stock goes live on the first of the current month; posted receipts, invoices, landed costs, returns and payments exist in the current month only; since the post-M4 routine pass each company also has a requisition turned into an order received and invoiced in two parts, an active blanket agreement with its first release, an invoice blocked by a price variance and an approved requisition waiting to be ordered, but there are no foreign-currency purchases in the demo yet; the seed adds under a minute to the roughly six of v3.
- **Demo seed v3 (3.9) boundaries**: the item master and the opening stock only; no movements after the opening date (receipts, shipments, transfers, counts and their documents arrive with the M4–M5 seeds), no BOMs, substitutes or item images; suppliers are the party references of v2 (partner masters land in M4); the seed takes about six minutes because every item goes through the item service and every unit through the posting engine, which posts one journal per item scope and costs about 25 ms per stock line in round trips (a bulk path would be faster but would bypass the rules; batching the engine's per-scope journals into one per document is a candidate improvement for M4, where receipts carry many lines).
- **Mobile scanning (3.8) boundaries**: the scanner is the web app installed as a PWA, not a native app (no background sync, no push; the queue replays while the page is open); camera decoding is best-effort (BarcodeDetector on Chromium/Android, ZXing elsewhere, and the typed input that hardware scanners use anyway) and is not covered by the browser journey, which drives the typed input; a capture sets the counted quantity of its line (the count API's semantics), it does not add to a previous capture of the same line; a code unknown to the device while offline is sent as typed and fails in the queue if the API cannot resolve it; the transfer flow is one-step between warehouses (no bins, lots or serials on transfer lines yet, no receiving flow); GS1 application identifiers (lot and expiry inside one barcode) are not parsed.
- **Replenishment (3.7) boundaries**: the planner is a reorder-point/min-max planner, not a forecast (demand forecasting is the intelligence milestone); incoming supply comes from approved purchase orders, and suggestions become draft purchase orders from the replenishment screen since the post-M4 routine pass (A-134); suggestions are per item and warehouse (no transfer suggestions between warehouses, no supplier minimum order quantities or pack rounding); the run is daily by schedule and on demand, without a per-item planning calendar.
- **Counts (3.6) boundaries**: count sheets are entered through the API or the mobile scanner (3.8); the variance value shown at review is an estimate at the item's average cost (the posted value comes from the costing engine); a count has one approver step, not the workflow engine (M6); a blocking count blocks the whole warehouse (or its bins) rather than only the counted items; the snapshot takes what the balances say and does not detect stock that was never booked anywhere but is found on the shelf except through a "found" entry.
- **Tracking (3.5) boundaries**: a lot's `consumed` status is not set automatically when its stock reaches zero (it stays `active` with nothing on hand); serial statuses `sold`, `returned`, `consigned` and the counterparties come from the `partnerId` the calling document passes (the sales and purchasing modules will, M4/M5; the tests pass ids); a serial in repair keeps its stock row on hold but there is no repair document (costs of a repair are a positive adjustment); FEFO is a suggestion and a refusal when no lot is named, not an automatic pick (the sales module will pick, M5); warranty dates and lot/serial custom fields are stored and editable but not used by any rule yet; the recall impact list names partner ids (partner masters land in M4/M5).
- **Stock documents (3.4) boundaries**: adjustment approval is a single approver (the company setting), not the workflow engine of ADR-0020 (which lands in M6 and will take these documents over); an assembly consumes its components at the posting date in one go (no work-in-progress, no partial builds, no disassembly); the assembly variance account is used only under standard cost; a shortage on a transfer receipt is written off at the transit warehouse's cost (there is no claim on the carrier or supplier yet). Under standard cost a revaluation writes the pool row (the standard itself is not changed by an NRV write-down).
- **Costing engine boundaries (3.3)**: the expected/actual split is kept per receipt, not carried through applications (COGS taken from an uninvoiced layer is reported as actual; the valuation's "expected" column is the uninvoiced receipts still on the books). A sale return is valued at the original shipment's cost only when the caller names the shipment entry (`appliesToSleId`; the sales module will, M5), otherwise at the current cost. Consigned stock is not valued; drop-ship pass-through waits for M4/M5. A re-application beyond the threshold defers the whole valuation of the triggering posting (its own value entries included) to the job, so the posting result shows a zero cost until the run completes. A run that cascades through transfers posts one journal per scope walk and GL date, so one trigger document can have several journals. The permutation property test covers company scope; warehouse scope is exercised by the transfer path only. The valuation report and the cost history are API-only until the M3 web pass. Lot and serial ids on a line are accepted as plain uuids and are validated (existence, expiry, FEFO) only when 3.5 lands. The `approve` negative-stock policy is treated as `block` until the approval workflow exists. In-transit quantity is derived from open transfers (no balance row for transit beyond the in-transit warehouse's own on hand). No `WAREHOUSE` dimension value is created for a warehouse yet (the dimension lands with the costing postings). No warehouse or stock screens in the web app yet: the module is driven through the API until the M3 web pass.
- **Item units are editable without a stock guard**: changing or removing a unit (or the base unit) is refused only by the item's own rules (base locked while other units exist, units used by bills of material kept); the guard against changing factors once stock has moved in that unit is still open (it belongs with the costing engine, 3.3, where a factor change would revalue layers). No item screens in the web app yet: the item master is driven through the API and CSV until the M3 web pass.
- **Audit anchoring defaults to the file store.** `Quicker:Audit:Anchoring:Store=object_lock` writes retained objects through `IObjectStorage` (S3 object lock in compliance mode with the `s3` provider; the `filesystem` provider only refuses through its own API, so on a single node the file store's hash-linked lines remain the tamper-evident choice). Anchoring and verification now run daily as the platform jobs `audit.anchor_all` (02:00 UTC) and `audit.verify_all` (03:00 UTC; the job fails, and is visible as such, when any chain is broken). Verification and export from the API still run synchronously inside a request (page size 2000, export capped at 50 000 rows).
- **Audit retention** (dropping partitions older than the configured period, default 10 years) is not implemented; nothing deletes audit data yet.
- **Backups are commands, not a schedule yet** (A-138, `docs/operations/backup-and-restore.md`): `backup`, `restore` and `verify` run from the migrator on demand; nothing takes a nightly dump, ships it to object storage with object lock, or archives WAL for point-in-time recovery (the managed database's PITR is assumed for SaaS, ADR-0024; the on-premise compose file with its nightly job is part of 10.3's installer, not built). The migrator's container image does not carry the PostgreSQL client tools yet, so the commands run from a machine or runner that has them (CI installs the PostgreSQL 17 client). A database backup holds neither attachment files nor audit anchors, which live in object storage and the anchor store.
- **Email is SMTP only.** `Quicker:Email:Provider=smtp` sends through any relay (Mailpit locally); the default `capture` keeps messages in memory and logs them. Provider APIs (SES, SendGrid, Postmark), bounce handling and per-tenant sender identities are not implemented; the identity module's invitation and reset emails still go straight to `IEmailSender`, not through the notification log.
- **Attachments are not scanned.** Uploads are limited by size and stored by hash, but there is no malware scan. Files left by a save that failed after its upload are swept daily once a day old (see the attachment sweep entry); the reverse case, a removal whose commit fails after its file was deleted, leaves a record without its file (the download says the content is missing). Files, like comments, follow the record's read permission (see the next entry).
- **Security keys are a second factor, not a password replacement yet**: they are registered on My account and used after the password at sign-in, and the browser journey runs a real WebAuthn ceremony (Chromium's virtual authenticator, attestation `none`) against the server; step-up takes a key too; passwordless sign-in with a passkey and attestation formats other than `none` (the server asks for none, so keys do not prove their make) are not built. A workspace that requires two-step verification enrols people without it through an authenticator app at sign-in; a key can be added afterwards.
- **Paging and filtering are applied where lists are unbounded** (notifications, activities, webhook deliveries, audit events) and the filter language to companies; other lists (companies, branches, roles, series, jobs, schedules) return whole arrays until they can grow, and each new list endpoint adopts `Page<T>` and a `FilterSpec` as it lands. `or` is not in the filter language (use `in`). Version ETags with `If-Match` are on companies; other resources have body-hash ETags (304 only) until they expose `updatedAt`.
- **Journals keep the payables subledger only** (A-140): a journal line on the payables control opens the supplier's open item and its reversal reverses it, and entries of document modules (purchasing, banking, payables, inventory) are reversed through their documents only. Journal lines on the other control accounts are not followed yet: the receivables control (no receivables module until M5), the bank control (the demo books bank movements by journal; bank transactions as documents are 6.1), and the inventory, goods-received and fixed-asset controls (their subledgers are value entries and assets, which a journal line cannot create). Such lines post as before and the harness reports any drift. Auto-reversal on `auto_reverse_on` is executed by the daily routine (`accounting.daily_routines`, 00:30 UTC) or on demand through `POST /accounting/routines/run`.
- **Posting-rule keys for tax codes, warehouses, bank accounts, asset categories and charge types are plain uuids** until their modules exist (M3–M6 add the foreign keys); item and partner posting groups and branches are validated today.
- **The Iraq statutory chart is group level and provisional** (nine groups of the Unified Accounting System; ASSUMPTIONS Q9): the mapping mechanism, the seeded reference table and the template mapping are complete, the detailed statutory codes below the groups are added once an Iraqi accountant validates them. Statutory reports themselves belong to M2.5/M6.
- **Accounting screens**: statutory mappings, chart CSV import/export, journal import and journal attachments (`entityType: manual_journal`, tenant-wide permissions, editable after posting) have screens since the post-M4 routine pass, as do posting profiles and rules, recurring templates and deferral schedules; each run of the daily routines is logged per company (see the routine run log entry). The journal editor takes account codes and amounts, and each line's details take its dimensions, supplier or customer and description (A-125); a bank control line's item is typed as a reference. The account editor refuses changes of meaning on accounts that already have lines (A-130). The trial balance screen filters on any number of dimensions at once, ANDed, matching what the API already took (see the multi-dimension filter entry below).
- **Stock postings do not store their source document's number** on the journal entry (the costing engine passes none, and it is left as it is in this pass): the entry, the journal browser and the ledger read the number from numbering when showing them, but the stored column stays empty, so a direct SQL or report over `gl_journal_entries` sees no number for those entries until the costing engine passes it. Document search lists numbers only; drafts (which have none yet) are found on their lists. Branch and warehouse scopes on a grant are not applied to search results (an allocation does not know its warehouse), matching the document lists, which do not apply them either.
- **Posting windows are per role or for everyone, not per user or per module**; a window applies to every module's postings through the engine (GL today) and owners are held to the company window like anyone else. The close checklist and year-end close of ADR-0026 are M6 work.
- **The invariant harness reads the ledger tables and the numbering tables directly** (read-only SQL, as the reporting layer does) rather than through each module's contract; the audit chain goes through `IAuditChainVerifier`. Its job is exercised through the handler with a platform job context, not through the worker loop (like the accounting daily routine).
- **The daily routine runs once a day at 00:30 UTC for every tenant**; a company whose day starts later posts its reversals and recurring journals with the date they carry, not the wall-clock date. A per-tenant schedule can be set in `ops.schedules` (payload `{asOf}` accepted). The job is exercised through the routine service and the endpoint; no test drives it through the worker loop yet.
- **Deferral schedules planned before a company's fiscal years exist fall back to calendar month ends** and keep `fiscal_period_id` empty; they post into whatever period covers the date at posting time. Recurring templates count runs from the cron only (no `every N periods` shorthand).
- **An account with postings keeps its meaning** (A-130): its type, whether it holds postings, its subledger reconciliation and any currency or company restriction its lines would break are refused on edit and on chart import; the balance moves to a new account by journal instead. Moving it under another header of the same type, renaming and deactivating stay open.
- **The demo tenant is purged physically on reseed**, audit chain included, through the owner connection in maintenance mode; that path exists only for the `demo` slug and the seeder, and no API or job deletes a tenant (tenant offboarding with retention is a later platform slice). Demo passwords are shared and printed by the command; a deployment that exposes the demo publicly should reseed it on a schedule.
- **Demo seed v2 stops at the general ledger**: its customers and suppliers exist only as subledger references on the AR and AP control accounts (fixed ids, bilingual names in the journal descriptions), and its sales, purchases, collections and payments are manual journals, not source documents; partner masters, items, warehouses and the documents that post through posting rules arrive with the seed versions of M3–M6, when the same references become real open items. Company, branch, chart, account and journal ids are assigned by the services, so they change on reseed while tenant, user, membership and party ids stay fixed. The seed posts as the system actor inside one transaction and takes about six minutes with the v3 stock (progress lines mark the phases); a reseed while the API is serving the demo tenant makes in-flight requests fail until it commits.
- **The API contract may still break before the first release tag**: the gate compares against `main`, and this branch's own history shows one intentional break (three lists became page envelopes). From the first tagged release on, A-082 applies without exception.
- **Every operation in the contract describes its success response** (a schema for bodies, `string`/`binary` for files, or a bodiless 202, 204 or redirect), and the contract check fails on any that does not, so the generated client stays typed end to end. The contract is one document for the whole API, not per tenant: custom fields are described by `GET /collaboration/custom-fields/{entityType}/schema`.
- **Logs reach the collector through stdout, not OTLP**: Serilog writes JSON to the console for the platform's log shipper (Loki in the local profile reads container output); the OTLP log signal is not wired (`Serilog.Sinks.OpenTelemetry` when a deployment needs it). Runtime and process metrics (GC, thread pool) are not exported yet; log levels are set at start, not adjustable at runtime; the per-tenant "system health" admin page (ADR-0025) reads the same gauges but is not built yet.
- **Rate limits are in-process per node** (sliding windows in memory; the Development settings allow 300 sign-in calls a minute per address instead of 20, because the browser journeys sign many people in from one address): a multi-node deployment multiplies the budget by the node count until a shared store (Redis) backs the limiter (1.11 hardening).
- **Dimensions on purchasing documents in the web app are on supplier invoices only**: requisition and order lines take a `dimensionSetId` through the API (and an invoice line inherits its order line's), but their web forms have no dimension pickers yet.
- **Custom fields are hosted by companies, items, partners, the six purchasing documents, manual journals, bank payments, stock adjustments and transfers, lots and serials**, with a form for each except lots and serials (their screens change status, they have no edit form yet). A required field applies to every save, including drafts the system creates for someone (orders from replenishment suggestions, payments from a payment proposal); give it a default value (A-137) and those drafts carry the default instead of being refused. Each module registers its table when it adds them; the OpenAPI document is not rewritten per tenant, clients read `GET /collaboration/custom-fields/{entityType}/schema` instead. Index DDL runs without `CONCURRENTLY` (fine while tables are small; the online variant is a follow-up before large tenants).
- **Comments, files, timelines and links follow the record's read permission and its company** (A-133, A-136): a member needs the record's module permission (for example `purchasing.order.read`) as well as the collaboration permission, held for the record's company when that permission is limited to some companies; items and partners are shared by every company of a workspace, so their discussion follows the permission alone. Warehouse and branch scopes do not narrow a record's discussion yet (a role limited to one warehouse sees the discussion of every stock document of its companies). A member can still be mentioned on a record they cannot read (they are notified, and the record stays closed to them). Saved views are per list, not per record.
- **Central Bank of Iraq rate adapter is not implemented.** The roadmap lists ECB, Open Exchange Rates and a CBI adapter; the first two ship (parsers covered by tests, import covered end to end against a local stub). The CBI page has no stable machine-readable feed I could verify from this environment, so the adapter is deferred rather than shipped untested; Iraq's official and market rates are entered manually or through a rate type an integration fills. Neither live feed has been called from this environment (no egress); the first production import should be watched.
- **Scheduled rate import** is available as the tenant job `organization.rates.import` (`PUT /api/v1/platform/schedules {code, jobType, cron, payload:{provider, rateType}}`); no tenant has one by default.
- **Fiscal year closing** (`status = closed`, closing entry, reopening a year) belongs to the Closing module (ADR-0026 year-end); today years are `future` or `open`, and period states are the only lock.
- **Company `chart_id` and `posting_profile_id`** are plain nullable columns until Accounting (M2) creates the tables they reference.
